## Volume 02, Batch 0002 — Chapters 60–70

### Story Progress

The batch takes the mechanism into a room and makes it cost somebody in public, and it does not defeat Schedule 4. It runs 1 May to 19 May 2014 in a calendar counted from Chapter 21 = Thursday 13 March.

**It opens on an object and closes on a hearing.** The Old Ward porch has nine plastic chairs numbered one to nine in chalky paint by a caretaker so that people who come every day can find the same chair, and four is worn through. *You number a chair. A chair is not a household.* On 19 May a committee that could not define the word *holder* put a registrar's record from April, a form with a hole in it, and a return with an unanswered ninth line into the minute, and that was the whole of what the city did in six hours.

**The instrument of visibility becomes an instrument of exposure, exactly as Nell Ardent warned on 1 April, and it happens to a person who did it to herself.** Nina Calder made the Return of a Deferred Node a public document on 7 May, having decided on the Sunday, because a trade association had a photocopy of the whole page with the departmental crest on it eight days after a clerk printed it for a records office. That afternoon Marguerite Oyelaran had a four-page circular with a paragraph number in it, a one-page letter from two colleagues of eleven years' standing, a councillor's office asking whether her association should be in the room at all, and a woman in a Methodist hall on her own road who had been waiting since Monday for a call. **She did not go into the hall.** *In three weeks I will say the fee does not stop, and it will not be true on Monday, because I will not have been in that hall.* A trade circular in eleven days; a queue of eleven in a corridor that had held none since January; **a man who had refused to sign because he did not understand the agreement coming up the stairs to sign, because his block is on a photocopy and being outside the thing is worse than being inside it.** Nina Calder's own sentence, said at the bench and not softened: **my form is how my plate got falsified inside four days by nobody doing anything wrong.**

**The batch's biggest fear arrives as a paperwork error, which is the correct way for it to arrive.** The tabling list said *Schedule 4 — referred on*; paragraph 2 of the covering submission asked the Committee **to approve and adopt it as a schedule of the Committee.** Nobody had moved either. The chair would not rule. And on 30 April at ten past five in the evening Nina Calder put Alden Rook's Wednesday-night stairwell prediction into the papers' background field in his words with his name and a date on it, because she will not state a true thing without saying whose it was, and it printed on Friday and cannot be taken out, and on 19 May the truest sentence anybody said all day was a prediction in a records supervisor's field.

**Four of the four nodes that Halvorsen had been carrying since Chapter 50 turn out not to be the problem he thought they were.** £4,200 buys Tier 2 on one node out of four, and on 10 May a morning and a half established that **a staff stair is not a service, a car park barrier is not a service, and a wet riser serving one room on level zero is a pipe in a building which the estates officer removed from the list himself in nine minutes under his own holding.** Nobody in the city had ever asked whether a thing on a list of things to pay for is a thing. So Tier 2 went to the mortuary van gate, unvoted, at twenty to one on the Saturday, by one man with a folder and three other men and a porter who did not know he had written the four words that made it expensive. **And because a schedule is a chain and a chain has two other things on it that are not nodes, the plate in a hospital corridor now says 6-41 IS ON A CONTINUITY SCHEDULE. TIER 2 — on a plate with no field for a schedule, no field for a tier and no field for money.** A hospital door is inside a priced thing and the door has nowhere to keep the price. Nobody has been billed. It expires at 06:00 on **Sunday 12 October 2014.**

### Midpoint

**Chapter 65. The Return is public, four blocks are on it, and the arithmetic Marguerite Oyelaran defended in a room on 28 April is legible to the hundred and twenty-eight people who have to pay it** — and a trade body has it, and a councillor has put in a council-chamber minute, unanswered by seven members, the question of whether the association should be in the room at all. And the twelfth withdrawal letter is hand-delivered face down because she would not put it in a box.

**The second half of the midpoint is the batch's arithmetic, and it is exact.** £2.60 a room a week is £135.20 a room a year; 122 paying rooms is £16,494.40; the schedule cost £16,800, so the shortfall is £305.60; **the cost of the six who would not sign is £811.20 a year to the penny — £505.60 of margin the association never had and £305.60 of hole it has now, all of it out of the same three hundred and five pounds sixty — and nobody in that office had ever worked it out.** At 28 April, on the page Marguerite Oyelaran handed over, it was 120 of 128 with a second sheet behind it carrying two more; by 2 May it was 122 of 128 and six had not signed. Block C is a void and going empty is £4,056 a year, and the shortfall goes to £4,361.60 against a void allowance that is nil in October, and what is still being collected off four blocks is £12,438.40. And the block's consumption went up thirty-one per cent in April, because all four blocks were full for eight days after the surge and none of them was empty for the eleven days in March the price was calculated on: **the fee is priced on a year in which these blocks are half empty and it is being paid for in a year in which they are full, and the difference is in four invoices to a hundred and twenty-eight people and is not in a letter.**

### Climax and Cost

**Chapter 70. Monday 19 May, committee room two, 10:00 to 16:10, gallery full at 10:55 with forty-one chairs and about thirty standing.**

**Nobody succeeded at beating a price with a sentence. It was attempted four times in three hours and it failed four times, and every failure was correct — and the third of the four was Marguerite Oyelaran, asked from the floor of the gallery at about 12:20 about a lift in a block of flats, giving four sentences, and a member replying that it was a matter for the association and not for this Committee, and her saying *yes it is*, and that being the end of it.** Alden Rook did not define *holder* — he never will, as he promised in a stairwell on 30 April — and made the volume's best argument: *the schedule does not price access to urgent care, it prices the continuity of a node; a care claim is not a node; the west clinic care entrance is not in failure and Schedule 4 did not touch it, and that door has been open every night since 2 April on a term read aloud at ten o'clock by two people who can say when it ends, and that is the only thing anybody in this city has done right since 3 April.* A member answered that this was therefore the finding. **And then a member asked whether the contractor's own glossary supplied a definition, and Ceri Vaughan offered page 460, sub-paragraph 3, which has been there since 1 March, in the spirit of not wasting anybody's time — and it was a good offer, and it was the best four seconds of the day, and it would have adopted a four-hundred-and-sixty-page schedule without a division.** **Oren Pell leaned forward an inch and advised the chair in eleven seconds: a definition given in the course of considering a document is a determination of that document.** The chair did not stop the man who wanted to move it, exactly as Rook had predicted; the mover withdrew it himself and asked that the reason be minuted, so that the Committee would not be said to have declined out of cowardice in six weeks. **The Committee recorded that it is unable to define the term *holder*, in the mover's own words.**

**Schedule 4 stands and is referred on.** The tabling discrepancy — *referred on* against *approve and adopt* — was recorded and not resolved, and the chair refused to rule because that is not a chair's job, and then disposed of it by noting it.

**The three things the city put into the record: a registrar's record of 18 April; a form with a hole in it, received into the Committee's records and not adopted; and a return whose ninth line has no answer in it.** The chair said the word *nobody* in the room in the first two minutes after the sitting resumed at two o'clock and **deliberately did not minute it as a word**, because a chair should not put a word into a minute that a records supervisor has not yet put into a form — and Nina Calder said it aloud from the second row of the gallery and said she would write it into the form on 20 May in her own words. **That is the origin of the 20 May coda and it belongs to her and not to the chair. The substance of it is in the record, though, and the next batch must not treat the word as absent from it: at ten past three the operative determination requires that where the answer to *who answers for it* is no party, that is to be recorded as no party, in those words.** The word is out of the chair's mouth and not in the minute at two o'clock; the rule about it is in the minute at ten past three. Neither the 2:00 exchange nor any 14:19 timestamp exists — do not cite a clock time for the word.

**The two determinations, at 15:10, are the only reason anybody will read the transcript in September.** The Return is **received**. And **every party holding continuity in this city is required to answer a Return of a Deferred Node, in public, on the form, by the next sitting, and the answers are to be read into the record as they are received; and where the answer to *who answers for it* is no party, that is to be recorded as no party, in those words.** And the operative sentence, and the mechanism of the whole hearing, and the chair's own condition: **a party that answers may be asked what it will do, and not what it will cost** — which she said is **not in the document, which she did not write, which she has had in her head since the second week of April, and which she cannot say where she got it from**, and which two members had assumed was on page nine. Underneath it, in the document, at page nine, is the opposite: **a tier may not be challenged by a person.** Her working of the pair: *a person is never in the way, and a claim nobody can answer is a claim nobody is ever in trouble about; a party that answers is in the way, permanently, and the moment that sentence exists in the record it exists in the mouth of whoever reads it out, which in this borough is apparently a woman with a form and a telephone number.* **Jonas, in the fourth seat back on the left, is the only person in that room who has read the sentence on a plate, and he did not say.**

**Jonas was in the gallery. He was not on the agenda, not a party, not a member, not an officer. He was asked nothing. He asked one question** — *is the Committee going to require somebody to answer for the six?* — **and the answer was no, on page nine, and the chair asked for it to be minuted that she had been asked to require a person and declined in the only words available.** The reason nobody has asked Marguerite Oyelaran is the reason the Committee cannot answer him. In September there is to be a public, minuted record of the nodes in this city that have **no party answering for them**, carrying no name, no address, no survey and no count of anything that lives anywhere. **Tomas Renn was not in the room. He asked Jonas on 27 April not to come, and Jonas did not come, and both facts are on the page.**

**The first transfer in the series that succeeds, and what it cost the man who received it.** On 17 May, in a chamber off the lowland road and a room in the civic offices and a garden in Tideglass at eight in the evening: **6-L/16's outfall inspection duty, moved from a held claim that lapsed at 23:40 on 29 April to Royden Achebe in his own name.** Jonas did the moving. Witness Emeka Rusk. **Second witness: NIL**, printed because it is the truth. **The claim is unpaid and the party that pays for the spur does not answer for it**, because the spur is on Tier 1 at £1,880 because CivicCore holds the standing continuity authorisation for a node whose maintenance CivicCore holds. **The instrument: a duty is not a place.** An outfall is not a water point; a duty is an act; an act can be held by a findable man in a findable office; **and the holding puts no address, no household and no person into a record, and nothing on it can be read and turned into a street.** It is not a recognition of a place or its people and must never become one.

**What it cost Royden Achebe:** the first thing the record did with the transfer was put Jonas's name on it as the man who moved it, and **Achebe required that cost to be written into the terms in Jonas's own words so that in August he can point at a piece of paper and not at a memory.** He offered the claim back inside the first hour of the seven-day window. **He cannot give it back, because an offer does not transfer a claim, and a claim is released only when a successor consents, and a person who has not been asked may not be named — so the only door out of it is to make somebody findable, and he is not going to do that.** *I would rather have had it on the twenty-ninth of April. In April I was refusing a thing. This week I am asking to give one back, and asking puts a date and a name and a shape on me, and this is the first one where the shape is me and the name is also me.*

**And the batch's last private cost, unpaid:** on Sunday 18 May at about half past ten, one of the six lowland water points is the stand at the top of a slipway, and a man wrote £1,880 a node on a piece of paper and gave it to a chair in a corridor at ten to eight the next morning, and **did not go up the path and did not telephone.** *That is the third time I have kept a promise in the exact way that breaks it.*

### Character Change

- **Jonas Mercer asked one question in a public room in eleven chapters and it was a number, and he had spent the number into a chair's hand at 07:50 the same morning so that when he said it he was reading a document that had existed since 28 April in somebody else's handwriting.** He refused a money question for the fourth time in five weeks; he performed no civic edit at all in the batch; he performed one transfer and did not issue the term; and he learned the whole of transfer stewardship in a single afternoon, which is that **the duty is the receiver's and the explanation is the mover's, and the difference is not a gift.**
- **Dr. Sanaa Iqbal** found £410 where the price had been £1,100, told him the price of it honestly (twenty minutes a week, in a corridor, on a rota with a hole in it), refused to be thanked in advance, and stopped him saying *noted*. **The June reckoning is Monday 9 June 2014 at ten in the morning, given as information and not as a request**, and the second clinician's line is suspended from 1 June. Nothing is reconciled.
- **Fenella Marsh** refused him the papers in a corridor in one sentence and it was not unkind, and on 19 May gave him a procedure instead of a refusal: *I cannot make anybody do anything about eleven thousand pounds. I can make sure that the sentence exists in a place where it can be read out.*
- **Marguerite Oyelaran** said the £305.60 out loud before he could, said the twelve were not a data set, said the eleven doors on the Tuesday morning were the first honest thing on that road since March, signed a man's name onto a page because his block was on a photocopy, and **did not go into a hall eleven households were living in at her expense.**
- **Priya Ranganathan** opened a hospital door for the first time in five weeks **and declared it in writing on the back of a departmental card in about seventy seconds, with the time, her own name, the bed and the receiving shift on it, read back to herself and entered by Mina Okafor, who did not say that it was the ninth one this month** — the plate's fallback requires the written declaration and she made it, which is the whole of the point and is why 9 June turns on the declaration structure. She refused to be thanked for turning up to her own job's witness requirement, and asked the question nobody had asked — what is a declining reduction — and then gave the answer nobody wanted: nobody is going to offer.
- **Gunnar Halvorsen** made the most consequential institutional act of the volume and could not have named his choice at eight that morning, and has a rule that asking a person about a date is how a person ends up on a rota, and has not asked anybody about anything. **As of Chapter 70 the suspension is announced and not carried out: on 8 May in a corridor he said he would write to the education office on the Monday morning and tell them the part-time on-call line is suspended from 1 June, and that he would tell Dr Iqbal himself, in person, in his office, and not by telephone — and the letter is not in this batch and the conversation is not in this batch. The line is suspended from 1 June in the record; what has happened on paper and in that room is owed to the next batch and must not be assumed.** He did it to keep four hundred and twenty women's instruments sterile.
- **Tomas Renn** has a date for his own question for the first time in four months and is going to ask it himself, in a corridor, and has told Jonas to stay out of a building on the 22nd and the 29th.
- **Nora Bell** took a seat and gave it back in four minutes and her name is on a roll she cannot get it off, and she gave a man with a laminated card a number she has kept in pencil for nineteen years and never gave anybody.
- **Alden Rook** kept his word, made the best argument in the room, was right about the chair and wrong about who would stop the definition, and paid £1,880 to schedule a pipe he maintains.
- **Nina Calder** made a document public to stop a document being traded, printed a second witness as NIL, refused the word *register*, put a true sentence in a field with somebody else's name on it, and said the word *nobody* out loud in a room and then wrote it into a form.

### Power and Continuity State

**Jonas ends Stage 2 with no civic edit performed in this batch and one transfer carried out.** He cannot define *holder*, cannot require a person, cannot take a burden back, cannot renew a term he did not issue, cannot be at the west site on 22 or 29 May by a shop steward's refusal, and **on 15 May he had the name for a mechanism he is not allowed to mend — a declining reduction, a cliff inside a cliff with no scale on the plate between them — and said two words and nothing else, because a stage-2 mending needs a person with local standing to instruct it and a named holder to take it, and there were four people in that corridor and not one of them was asking him for anything.**

**The body cost is unchanged:** seam mark past the elbow into the upper arm, right hand numb to the elbow and will not close, a roofing slate and a forearm for the whole of a mend.

**Four Lattice notices across eleven chapters, at most one each, in Chapters 61, 65, 68 and 70. None of them defines *holder*.** The Ch 46 ceiling was not repeated.

### New canon this batch

- **The 19 May outcome in exact wording** (defined holder not; Schedule 4 stands and referred on; the tabling discrepancy recorded and unresolved; the Return received and not adopted; every party holding continuity required to answer one in public; no-party answers to be recorded in those words; Sore's arithmetic motion; the chair's sentence about being asked what it will do and not what it will cost).
- **Saint Orra west: Tier 2 £4,200 on 6-44, effective 11 May, to 06:00 Sunday 12 October 2014; three of the four nodes were not services; the care entrance is on the schedule on a plate with no field for the price; nobody has been billed; the mortuary gate has been open since 00:01 on 26 April on a night orderly's four words in biro. THE 4 OCTOBER TERM IS THE LAST ONE THAT EXISTS ENTIRELY INSIDE A FUNDED SCHEDULE AND IT ENDS AT THE EXACT MINUTE THE SCHEDULE EXPIRES, AND THE 22:00 HANDOVER ON SATURDAY 11 OCTOBER READS A TERM THAT BEGINS ONE HOUR AFTER THE MONEY STOPS, AND NOBODY IN THAT CORRIDOR KNOWS.**
- **Halvorsen's decision, who he wrote to, and his rule about asking: every time he asks one of those four people about a date, one of them ends up on a rota because of the asking.**
- **CivicCore placed 6-L/16 the lowland spur on Tier 1 at £1,880 on 12 May, and the outfall inspection duty fell out of it on 29 April.**
- **The first successful transfer, its receiving holder (Royden Achebe, in his own name), the record that names Jonas as the mover, the NIL second witness, the unpaid claim, the seven-day offer that does not transfer, and the sentence *this is the first one where the shape is me and the name is also me*.**
- **A duty is not a place** — the batch's instrument, and the answer to a plate that will not take a bare role, and explicitly not a recognition of a street or its people.
- **The council flood forum's roll, that it is a list, that Nora Bell's name is on it, and the empty eleventh place that is not written *vacant*.**
- **Marguerite Oyelaran's withdrawal numbers (twelve by 3 May, eleven of them on one Tuesday morning, the twelfth letter hand-delivered) and the Block C outcome (a void, £4,056, shortfall £4,361.60, isolation on 6 May).**
- **£305.60, £16,494.40, £17,305.60, £4,056, £4,361.60, £16,800, £1,880, £4,200, £11,280, £410, £1,100, £913, 940, 122 of 128, 19 of 31 (untouched), 128, 30, 41, 61, 1,100, 2,400, 480 m, 6-41, 6-44, 6-L/16.**
- **The June reckoning's date: Monday 9 June 2014, ten in the morning, a review of the west clinic's own witness structure, chaired by Sanaa Iqbal, nine people, four of them on that roster, and he is not on it.**
- **What Priya Ranganathan did on 15 May and what she asked.**
- **The Borough Heat Threshold Protocol**, pinned to the Old Ward board, activating on three consecutive days at 27°C and requiring residential care premises to confirm occupancy and a named responsible person for every address in their custody.
- **The 12 October problem**, dated, live, and unanswered, and sharper after Ch 67: the term read on Saturday 4 October is the last one in that building that exists entirely inside a funded schedule and it ends at the exact minute the schedule expires; the renewal due on Saturday 11 October reads a term that begins 07:00 on 12 October, one hour after the money stops; so the 11 October handover is the crossing, it is a Saturday night, and if it does not happen the door is on nothing at all.

### Handoff

**The next batch is Chapters 71–80 — the heat.** **12 October 2014 is a Sunday, a hospital door is inside a priced thing until it, and the 22:00 handover on Saturday the eleventh is the hour the door crosses, read by two tired porters who will not know what they are doing.** The batch must open on or after 20 May and before the end of June. The Borough Heat Threshold Protocol is on the board, the forecast is 30, 31, 29, 28, the spring tides are 21, 22, 28, 29 May and 1 June, and **Achebe's 6-L/14 report is due 29 May and will land in the same week.** On 22 and 29 May Tomas Renn is going to ask the two-refusal question in a corridor at Saint Orra west and **Jonas has been told twice not to come and has not been invited.** The second clinician's line stops on 31 May. The clinic's own witness-structure review is **Monday 9 June** and he is not on it. **Margo Doré has not been told that a Tier 1 price on six lowland water points is in a public record in the Old Ward.** The cooperative steward board was named and refused in one sentence on 19 May and does not exist.

Open threads: the heat and what a heat protocol asks a landlord for · **the 11 October handover, which is the crossing** · the two-refusal rule, now dated to the eleventh · the first transfer that has to be repeated, and who rings him in August · the September record of nodes with nobody answering for them · the 122 who signed and the twelve who want out and the man who signed on 8 May · Block C's water on 6 May · the decontamination room's twenty minutes a week with nobody against it · Priya Ranganathan's two days a week and a daughter of eleven · Margo Doré, unpaid · the Weir, untouched, with no pump offered and the trap lost for good.

**Unspent and forbidden in Volume 02:** the Quiet Cut, the civic erasure, the origin of the watch term, the three-line brass tokens, Evan's counter-amendment and second signature, Iona, the root chamber, the blank bearer, the name in biro on the map, the boy of nineteen on the bicycle, the letter burnt in the cook-house stove, the Weir's three positions, **and the nineteen of thirty-one at Wren Court, which no chapter in this batch touched and which may not be resolved before Volume 03.**

## Volume 02, Batch 0003 — Chapters 71–80

### Story Progress

The volume takes the schedule into weather. Section C opens on the notice already pinned to the Old Ward board and spends ten chapters establishing **what the heat protocol measures, who it asks, and what it costs, and then shows that the answer to all three is that nobody can find out, and that a document everybody trusts turns out to describe a weather station four miles away on the saltmarsh.**

**The batch's structure is a ladder of forms, and every rung is one rung lower than the last.** The heat protocol asks a landlord for a *state* and hands her a *number box*; the number that went in is 106. **128 rooms · 122 on the rent roll · 106 occupied · 92 rooms the association is actually paid for.** The only difference the two public numbers show anybody is **sixteen — sixteen rooms on a rent roll with nobody in them, which is a void, which is a landlord's exposure figure, which reads as bad management, and which the chief officer names as her own fault before anybody else does.** 106 minus 92 is **fourteen**, and eight of those are the eight households in Block C who have been carrying the water for thirty rooms in buckets since 1998 and six are the six who never signed, and **those are the only two numbers in the whole business that anybody in this borough would recognise as hardship and the only two that cannot be on anything, because every document that could hold them is a list of who does not pay.** The notice that printed on the plate about a service narrowed for a reason of season is true of a block that has had no water for a fortnight and will be quoted about a full one in September. The registrar's record of April and a service manager's report of May sit in two drawers in two buildings on two files about the same bank, and **no rule anywhere requires anybody to read both.**

**The batch's central beat is paid, once, in daylight, and it is paid by a man doing nothing.** On Tuesday 26 May at ten in the morning, at a table, with a form and a crest on it, Gunnar Halvorsen spends £1,880 of an activity budget of £2,140 to put a tank feed on Tier 1, and he says twice that it is the only door in the room, and he is right, and everybody in the building agrees he is right. **Jonas is not asked, says nothing, signs nothing including the witness line, and goes and reads his plate. Bea Osunde gives him one sentence: *I have watched you for four months and you have never once been in a position where doing nothing was the correct thing and I have not got a single useful thing to say about it.*** He does not narrate the lesson and does not use the word. The Section B recognition debt is therefore paid here and **may not be paid again**; Card 0078 was used for the batch's second refusal instead.

**The other half of the batch is that everything works.** A hospital gets its water back in eight hours and a stated time after that. A block that has had no water for a month still has no water while £4,200 a year is paid for the continuity of its node. A rota that does not exist gets a finding, and the finding is that a rota does not exist.

### Midpoint

**Chapter 75.** The mechanism does exactly what it has always done and it is correct. A node in failure is deferred; a deferred node serves its last verified essential use; if none is recorded, the use is the node's maintenance. **In a heat a node's maintenance is a bigger claim than it was in April, and nobody has re-priced anything, because the schedule prices the node and not the summer.** The six lowland points are in their fifth week on a trickle, one of them is the Weir stand, and its maintenance is a pump in a silt trap that cannot be cleared by anybody, ever — and in thirty-one degrees a trap is the only thing anybody on that street wants, and a man says so to Jonas Mercer in eleven words on a lowland and Jonas does not offer anybody a pump.

### Climax and Cost

**Chapter 80, the June reckoning, Monday 9 June at ten in the morning.** Nine people, four of them on the west roster, one of whom has been un-funded since 1 June. **Dr Sanaa Iqbal chairs it, does not defend her own design, and gives the other half of the finding before the finding, and the other half is two dates and not one. **The forty hours are the failure and she says so first:** a clause needing two people in a corridor at ten at night went forty hours without either of them, 06:00 Tuesday 15 April to 22:00 Wednesday 16 April 2014, with a woman of eighty-four in bay six for the whole of it, a man of eighty-one who wanted his own sheets, and about forty other people, and she signed the clause. **And on the night of Wednesday 23 April 2014, eight days later, the same structure did the other thing:** a mortuary gate opened at about 23:40 because a night orderly of twenty-nine who was not on anybody's rota wrote four words in nine seconds in biro on the back of a laundry delivery note, and a registrar of sixty-one declined to strike them in ninety seconds on a telephone because striking them would have emptied the field and the maintenance on a mortuary gate is a man with a key. Not despite — because of. **A witness arrangement is a promise made by people who are tired at ten at night, and it is the only kind of promise anybody in this city has been able to make, and a person who has read only the fifteenth of April has worked out nothing at all.** The finding: *the renewal clause has not failed through drafting, it has failed through the absence of a rota; a practice of unpaid, unscheduled, voluntary witnessing held a care entrance open and is not a service and cannot be made into one by any instrument held by the borough, the contractor, or any person; the third time a surge breaks this, a schedule will be written, it will be a good schedule, and it will be adopted.*

**The batch's deepest refusal is hers and it is the finding.** The decontamination verification log on level zero has carried no signature since 9 May and the weekly twenty minutes has not happened once, and the supplier's quarterly visit is in July, and a non-conformance has a paragraph number in front of four hundred and twenty women. **She could sign it. She said so. She did not, and gave the reason: if her name is against twenty minutes a week, that person is a doctor, and she is the one already in the building at eleven at night, and then it is a rota with one name on it, and a rota with one name on it is what everybody has been refusing for four months, and Bram's Thursday is a rota with one name on it and he asked for it to be left in exactly those words. Both of those refusals are the failure and neither is cowardice.** Yvonne Amoah, minuted: *that is the first time in eleven years I have heard a doctor say I could do it and then not do it.*

### Character Change

- **Jonas Mercer** learns that being the only person anybody in this city will say yes to is not a skill and is not a service, and that the price of being it is four people who are worse off and a fifth who is thanked for something he did not do. **He brings nothing to a room in June, and he asked one question in a room in June, and it was about a steriliser log and not about himself.** He goes up a path on a Sunday unasked with nothing to do and is told, in a kitchen, that he has not done it again and has done it worse, and is not angry with him, and that the two of those are not the same thing.
- **Marguerite Oyelaran** loses Block C, a quarter of a return, and a whole afternoon, and says the fee does not stop once, in front of about forty people, and tells them there are twelve and does not tell them it is a number, and refuses three times in one week to give the breakdown of sixteen, and says the sentence she has carried since 28 April: *I do not know which of the two I am more afraid of.*
- **Gunnar Halvorsen** chooses a price in daylight, for good reasons, and calls it the only door in the room, and is right, and is thanked for it by nobody, because in that city being thanked for buying something is the same as being told the sale was a favour. He tells a doctor about a suspended line on foot in daylight with the paper, is not thanked, and has a decision on a Thursday with £260 behind it.
- **Royden Achebe** files a correct report about a piece of ground he cannot reach, cannot release the burden, names a duty he holds in a different file that nobody has to read with this one, and knows that in August he is going to ring a man about a Tuesday.
- **Emeka Rusk** refuses a duty he has been doing unpaid for a month, in three correct reasons, and asks for the thing to be recorded and not for it to go on a form.
- **Margo Doré** refuses a pump in advance, in her own words, in a kitchen, to a man who came for nothing and not to a pump, and nobody argues, and no pump is offered and none exists.
- **Bram Osei** says one thing in eighty minutes and it is that he would rather be the bad refusal on a wall than part of a good one on a form.
- **Priya Ranganathan** asks for her name to stay on the record of a line that no longer exists, and says why in a way that will still be true in two years.
- **Oren Pell** says the best thing he says in the volume and it is the sentence that stops a board being formed: a steward board whose ninth seat cannot be challenged is not a steward board, it is a register with chairs.

### Power and Continuity State

**Jonas Mercer ends the batch Stage 2, unchanged in capability, and with his name on a second file.** He performed no civic edit. He attempted one repeat transfer and the receiving person refused it and the registrar ruled that the record has no field for what actually happened and that the entry is made twice and nobody is blamed. **Nina Calder put `moved by: MERCER, J.` on that second entry against his objection and read it back to him before typing it, and told him why: the two entries are incompatible, the file will not know the difference, and he will not be able to prove it is not true.** Legal responsibility follows the transfer and not the person who received it, and the man who accepted it knows he is going to ring in August.

**The volume's instruments are now four:** continuity tiering and its order · deferral, not disconnection, narrowing to a last verified essential use or to maintenance · **a duty is an act and not a place, and a duty cannot be half-shared** · rule 4(c). **And a fifth condition, printed in June: a determination with a deadline and no date on it is not a deadline, and the obligation is discharged by the act of answering and not by anybody receiving the answers.**

**Saint Orra west:** 6-51 is on Tier 1 from 1 June at £1,880 out of £2,140. The 11:00–13:30 gap is still published and unfilled. Bram's refused Thursday is on the board. Priya's line is un-funded from 1 June and her name is staying on the record. The twenty minutes a week on the decontamination log has nobody against it and has had since 9 May. The terms are still six days. The gate still runs on its local term and cannot be renewed by Jonas, the department, or a manager. **The 11 October crossing is untouched.**

**The cooperative steward board was named twice and refused twice and was not formed.** Section 11 of the 2008 continuity regulations exists and Frances Tolley has a copy in her own hand. **The condition has been stated and not answered: for nine seats to be anything other than nine chairs, one of the nine has to be able to be put in a room and told to stop, and removed in front of the other eight, by people who did not put him there.**

### New canon this batch

- **The Borough Heat Threshold Protocol**, its definition of *residential care premise*, its one station four miles away, its activation on 26 May 2014, its return date of 4 August 2014, and the fact that the Carrow Lane Housing Association is inside it because of a gas inspection in 2011.
- **Block C's whole state**: 1938, thirty rooms, riser dead since February 1998, eight households carrying the water in buckets and never in a letter, twenty-two households in a hostel and two flats, no lock and no key and a tag that says 1998, the freeholder's four meters, a node still on Tier 2 with no water behind it, and the fee that has not been stopped for a reason that is not moral.
- **The 106**: what a party that has been asked for a state does when it is handed a number box, and what two legible numbers do to six unnamed households.
- **Eleven Thursday handovers**, counted once, on purpose, out loud, in a corridor.
- **The transfer that had to be repeated**, the registrar's eleven seconds, and the second entry with his name on it.
- **The 6-L/14 report**: correct, unpaid, unreleasable, nameless, and pointing at a duty held in another file.
- **The 9 June finding**, word for word, and the refusal of the twenty minutes a week, and the reason the refusal is the finding.
- **Nina Calder's 2011 sentence**, the man from estates, and a fortnight with a bucket under a tap.
- **Thursday 19 June 2014**, the next sitting, and how it got its date.

### Handoff

**The next batch is Chapters 81–90 — Section D, Revocation, the volume's climax.** It must find the chain, find the node with a person in its holder field, get that person to instruct the mending, and refuse to do it himself. **The cost is that revocation has to be paid for somewhere, and it is paid for at Wren Court, where the fee stops and Yolanda Fofana's third condition — the fee does not stop — has to be renegotiated by the people it protects, in a room, without Jonas in it. Wren Court's nineteen of thirty-one is untouched after thirty-two chapters and Section D is the last place it can be paid before Volume 03, and the prompt must say clearly whether it is touched or not.**

**Lived on: 19 June is a fixed date with a folder and a clerk and fourteen returns and a chair who will not arrange a definition. A heat protocol is activated with a return date of 4 August. A decontamination log has no signature and a quarterly visit in July and four hundred and twenty women. A service manager has a decision on a Thursday with £260. A registrar's level-three tray will produce a signature in about eleven weeks on a line that says not maintainable and not released. A block of 1938 with no water and a stopcock that turns, and eight households who have asked for a letter twice in sixteen years and have not got it. Fourteen households inside a difference of fourteen that is on no document, and sixteen empty rooms inside a difference of sixteen that is. Six households who never signed, two of whom have told their landlord to her face they will not sign a thing that makes her name a door. A Sunday in October nobody has put in front of a board. A Tuesday in August that a man in Tideglass knows about and a man in River Stacks has not decided about. And a woman on a lowland who has refused a pump in advance and is not angry with him and has not forgiven him and was not asked.**

## Volume 02, Batch 0004 — Chapters 81–90 (Section D — Revocation)

### What happened

**The chain, found on the page, in a room, by a clerk.** Thursday 19 June 2014, ten in the morning, committee room two: a determinations meeting on fourteen returns, everything correct, and a chair reading into the record two answers about one meter room that cannot both be the answer. The determination of 19 May said *read as received*, and as received they went in, and Frances Tolley, clerk of twenty-two years, put two pieces of paper next to each other at ten past one and found a claim reference in two different places on two different forms. `T3/11400/0304`. The volume stopped being about a price list and became about a chain. **The chain is: CivicCore (placed, on a power of attorney, 4 March 2014) → the Aldbrook Trust (assigned whole, 21 March) → the Halliwell Amenities Fund, one-half (2 May) → the Salter's Yard managing agent, Beverley Annis (27 May, £600) → the named steward of the building (entered 3 June, by nobody asking her, on the strength of a plate in a meter cupboard).** The node is in Wren Court, Salter's Yard, Tideglass, and it is not a water node and it is not at Saint Orra west.

**The method, stated aloud for the first time in public, by Oren Pell:** *a price is a statement about what a thing costs; a chain is a statement about who is standing behind the price; the Committee cannot unmake a price and should not try; what it can do is name the chain in public and put the last person in it into a position where somebody has to ask her a question.* And **Alden Rook answered it and was not wrong about any of it**: the Committee has built a method whose last step is the one step page nine forbids it to take, and that is the method working; and if the chain is broken in the middle, ninety days' notice revives the whole of it at Tier 3 on **Wednesday 1 October 2014**, with no power in this city able to stop it.

**The person at the end of it.** Yolanda Fofana, 54, named steward of Wren Court, findable since 7 May and not asked. She learned what she was holding **on Monday 23 June at about a quarter past two, from a form with a crest on it, telephoning a records counter, and the counter told her the truth because the counter is required to.** She read all three assignments herself out of a public deed register, with the placing, on four sheets in her own hand, copied by a records clerk who said she did not trust herself not to improve them.

**The refusal.** Jonas Mercer was asked, once, on a step, on Friday 27 June at twenty-seven minutes past two: *what will you do.* She did not say yes. She said she would say it twice, and that the second time would be in front of thirty-one people. On Monday 30 June he refused in a workshop to be the mender — *the man who found a chain is not the man who ends it* — and on Tuesday 1 July, in a meter cupboard under a stair, with a tool roll he never opened, he said it out loud to nobody and refused again, in his own words, at length. **Pell ruled in one paragraph: where the man who found the chain is also the mender, the standing must be given twice, aloud, in the room, after the chain is known, and the second time is the one that is entered.** The second time was given on Wednesday 2 July at eleven at night, in the workshop, to twenty-nine people. The entry was made Thursday 3 July at nine. The term ran to **06:00 Friday 4 July**.

**The cost, in a form, on a public board, with one name on it and the name is his.** **One day and seven hours** (11:00 Wed 2 July, the second standing, to 06:00 Fri 4 July) — the tail of a total delay of **three days and twenty hours** from 10:00 Monday 30 June, of which **one day and twenty-three hours is his** and the last day and seven hours is a term no power can shorten. £2,431.16 out of a workshop. A riser that is two years away. A node at Kestrel Hill left at Tier 2 with a term to 4 August. A minute with two entries for one meter room that cannot be corrected. A return due again on 4 August. And a **ninety-day notice** served on five parties at 11:00 on **Thursday 3 July**, the day of the entry, which **expires Wednesday 1 October**.

**Nina Calder's fourth page.** She put it in the bin tray on Friday 4 July at twenty past four, and took it out on Monday 7 July at about half past eight, and wrote on it. It is not a list: **one week, one place, one number, what was counted, and one sentence saying what it is not — and a person may refuse to be counted, and the count then says how many refused.** It is the closest thing to an instrument that works anybody in this city has designed since March, and she said so, and then said she had a fifth one in her head and was not writing it down because writing it down makes it a scheme.

**Payment, at Wren Court, in a room Jonas Mercer was not in, over three evenings, 2–4 July.** The fee did not stop. It is thirteen pounds sixty, into the same tin, and nothing was signed and there is no minute. The seventy-two pounds a month stopped, and two thousand four hundred and thirty-one pounds and sixteen pence stopped coming out of a workshop, and the water went back to exactly what it was in April, and the riser is about nine hundred pounds and is two years away, and nobody in that room can raise it without producing a list of who can. Clause nine was read out in full four times and not repealed, not suspended, not rewritten. The twelve were not asked to sign. The four who missed a month were told, in front of everybody, and not written down. And thirty-one people who are not a body arrived, in four sentences, at the thing the whole volume has been walking towards: **one person, findable, holding a thing she cannot be asked about by any of the thirty-one people whose water it is.** Fofana said the third condition is not hers any more and she is not going to be thanked for it.

**The batch's last honest act** was a night orderly of twenty-nine refusing to be thanked in a stairwell at Saint Orra west at ten past ten on Wednesday 16 July — *if you thank me I am a person in a record, and a person in a record is a person somebody can ask* — and, eleven minutes later, a card-table clerk of nine years asking whether the practice can be written down and being told by the ambulance duty officer that writing it down is the beginning of it being made into a service, and that the record exists and is a record and not a rota, and that the only nine people who have read the 9 June finding are the nine who were in the room, and that nobody in that building has told the people doing the practice what is in it, and that this is the worst correct thing anybody in this city has done this year.

**Two refusals of numbers, both in July.** Jonas Mercer refused to manufacture a count of the nodes with nobody answering for it, in front of a man writing a report with a deadline, when he could have made one in about four minutes out of a public record. A woman telephoned the shop on Friday 11 July at twenty-five past four, gave no name, said *I am one of the eight*, and said the letter has been asked for twice and nobody has written it. He did not ring the number back.

### Numbers that changed something

`T3/11400/0304` · Tier 3 £11,400 · the block's share **£2,850 a year plus a £30 registration charge = £2,880 a year, charged at £72 a month** · four charges of £72 since 11 March, **£2,431.16** short on the block's water account and paid by the steward · tin **19 × £13.60 × 52 = £13,436.80 a year** (was 19 × £11 × 52 = £10,868) · riser about **£900**, two years away · **one day and seven hours** (11:00 Wed 2 July to 06:00 Fri 4 July), out of a total delay of **three days and twenty hours** from 10:00 Mon 30 June, of which **one day and twenty-three hours is his** · **ninety days** from service at 11:00 on **Thursday 3 July 2014** to **Wednesday 1 October 2014** · the register of narrowings at **fifteen** entries on 18 July, fourteen of them on 1 June · KH-4/20 at Tier 2 £4,200 to 06:00 **Monday 4 August 2014** · 19 signed / 12 unsigned / 4 lapsed / 11 in the building on 17 April · 29, 31, 31 in the room · 940 pencil marks, which are times and not people.

### Lattice notices

**Two printed, two restated — four in the batch, at most one per chapter, none defining *holder*.**
- Ch 82, the Vale Clinic gatepost, Kestrel Hill (new): `WHERE A CLAIM IS DISCHARGED, THE CLAIM IS DISCHARGED FOR THE PERSON WHO HELD IT AND NOT FOR THE PERSON WHO CAUSED IT, AND THE PERSON WHO CAUSED IT MAY BE FINDABLE AND IS NOT THEREBY ANSWERABLE.`
- Ch 85, the meter-cupboard plate at Wren Court, beside the existing 1940s line (new): `A THING THAT A PERSON IS ASKED TO DO BECAUSE A RECORD DOES NOT HAVE A FIELD FOR IT IS STILL A THING THAT PERSON IS ASKED TO DO.`
- Ch 89, **spoken by Jonas Mercer in full in dialogue**, quoting Ch 76's condition from memory and adding *that is a plate, and it is a condition, and it is not advice, and you can do exactly what it says*. **Decision recorded after review: a condition restated in speech is a restatement and not a printed notice, and the printed-notice budget counts text that appears on a plate, a board, a panel or a form. Ch 89 therefore costs nothing against the four.** It is the fullest statement of that condition anywhere in the volume, and the next batch may not add a fuller one.
- Ch 90, the Old Ward board, **a truncated and explicitly-marked** restatement of the same Ch 76 condition, cut at *not for a list*, and the truncation is stated on the page.

### Untouched, as required

No death, no rescue by chance, no lucky timing. No root reading, root clause, succession, Iona, Evan, or Quiet Cut. No 48-minute figure anywhere. The two-refusal date is **Saturday 11 October 2014** and it is not resolved and not staged again. The Weir, the annex, the retainer, the burnt letter, the boy of nineteen, the map's margin, the blank line, the claim at 6-L/14, Dev Singh's empty-crossing clause, the Saint Orra west rota, Prue Calloway's location-of-record finding, and Margo Doré's advance refusal of a pump. **The cooperative steward board was mentioned once, in a corridor, by a registrar, and was not constituted, staffed, minuted or promised.** The claims box closed on Friday 18 July with the one card about a stair sealed into an envelope and **nothing written into it**.

### Lived on

A chain of four valid documents, which is one placing and three assignments, and a torch in a cupboard, broken in the middle and not at the end. A registrar who has made four rulings that are doors and not walls and is tired since the eleventh of June. A woman on a landing who is going to keep holding a thing nobody can ask her about, for nothing, until she is sixty-one. A records supervisor who put a page in a bin and took it out. A night orderly who would rather be the bad refusal than a name in a field. A man who wrote the cost of the best thing he has done this year on a public form with his own name on it and one other name deliberately left out. A chalk board in Gantry Ward with a nine-millimetre ghost on it that reads TIDEGLASS 3 and that nobody rubbed out. A decontamination log with fifty-two empty signature boxes and a non-conformance raised on Thursday 17 July. A letter owed to eight households since about 2008, asked for twice, and a telephone call about it that gave no name. And two dates on a wall that belong to other people: the fourth of August, and the eleventh of October.

### Handoff

**The next batch is Chapters 91–100 — Section E, Chapters 91–96 as the volume outline fixes them, and then the volume's close.** Section E must rule priority access invalid in a public case, must leave CivicCore holding the physical network, must see a cooperative steward board FORM with nine seats, and **may not spend anything Batch 0004 has not already paid.** Live and carried: the first of October; the 4 August heat return; the decontamination non-conformance and the autumn compliance report; the two dates; the 6-L/14 tray due about 13 August; Nina Calder's fourth page and the fifth one she has not written; and the condition one of nine has to be able to be put in a room and told to stop, which is still unanswered.

---

## Volume 02, Batch 0005 — Chapters 91–100 (Section E — The Floor, and the close of the volume)

### What happened

**The volume opened with two dates arriving at once and neither of them making a noise, and it closed with two dates written in biro on a bench.** On Monday 4 August at 06:00 the heat-protocol return fell due and a private clinic's residential floor in Kestrel Hill went off a schedule, and by the end of the batch **that floor is a stair, a door and a lift, and it works because a man of sixty-one put a fire door wedge on the floor in May, and nobody has told the freeholder, and the freeholder has a notice in the post and does not know it.**

**THE 4 AUGUST RETURN. The number that went in the ring-number box is NINETY-TWO.** Deliberate, chosen by a chief officer who has read about the first one, entered on the Friday, public on completion. 106 in May, 92 in August, both legible to the borough since the day they were written. **The difference between them is a number and the difference is not a household, and there is no instrument in this city that can turn a difference into a household, and that is the only reason nobody is hurt by it this week and the reason somebody is going to be hurt by it in about four months and nobody can say who.** 122 on the rent roll, so the Block C void of thirty rooms is now publicly legible as a difference. Fourteen — the only figure in this business anybody in this borough would recognise as hardship — is a difference and not a figure and is on no instrument and cannot be put on one. She chose it. **He has still not telephoned her. That is the fifth time and both of them have now said in public that it is permanent.**

**THE DECONTAMINATION LOG. The batch's refusal and the batch's payment are the same object and neither one is Jonas Mercer.** On 5 August he stood in a decontamination room on level zero, had a pen within ninety seconds of his hand, did not sign and did not do the twenty minutes, and said why out loud to a bench: **filling it in is a service, and once there is a name against twenty minutes a week, twenty minutes a week has got a person on it, and a rota with one name on it is exactly what this building has been refusing since March, and he has spent five months saying out loud that the practice cannot be made a service and he is not going to be the man who signs the first line of one.** On 15 August **Ronan Vasquez, 48, estates, eleven years, not a doctor, not on a rota, not clinical, signed it at 16:20 in a flat sheet in a plastic wallet having thought about it since the previous afternoon.** He put three things on the form in his own words: it is not a service, it is a person reading a strip; he has held the maintenance of that room's pipework since 2011; **if anybody makes this a rota he will stop and they can have the paragraph number instead.** He said his name goes on a log and does not go on the corridor board, and that both of those are true and neither cancels the other, and that he will do it until the end of October and that after October it will lapse, because a job in November has a department in it. **Nobody asked him anything. That is the whole of the reason and the chapter says so.** **Nina Calder's rider went onto the second page of the acceptance — the fourth line of the 9 June finding, copied word for word, unamended and unsummarised, into a compliance document for the first time in this city, because Fiona Grieve said in June that it had to be and nobody did it until somebody else had signed.** The supplier granted the forward half and could not grant the historical half, because a compliance record that cannot be reconstructed is not reconstructed. **The non-conformance carries forward and is on the annual compliance report in the autumn with its paragraph number, in front of four hundred and twenty women, and it will say the named person is not on a rota.** Yvonne Amoah: *it was not a rota on the Wednesday, it is a paragraph in the autumn, and that is worse, because you can appeal a rota and you cannot appeal a paragraph.*

**THE PUBLIC CASE. Thursday 28 August, a registrar's public determination, a transcript, nineteen in the gallery, an unchecked press list.** The Carrow Lane Housing Association applied against its own interest, told nobody, and put it on a board in a shop. **Rook did not contest a fact, asked for that to be minuted before she started, and made the volume's best argument: we found out; that is the whole of my case and the whole of my weakness; a paid claim is a more trustworthy claim only because the payer found out, and that is a true sentence and a terrible reason to price a door.** She did not ask for her money back and gave the reason into the record: **£4,200 would not have fixed a riser, and she worked that out in a block in April and had nobody to give it to.** She described six words written in a records room in eleven minutes in March and accepted by a panel on 1 April, and said she has never found out who wrote them and is not going to.

> **THE RULING, and it is the whole of what the volume won: where the order of precedence between two nodes is set by the amount of a continuity fee paid, and by no other criterion, the tier so acquired is not a determination of priority. Made on a sub-substitution of the Schedule, limited to precedence, and it does not affect the validity of the Schedule, any placement, the maintenance of any node, the physical works of any party, any fee paid before this date, or the notice served on the third of July. Nothing in it requires any person to do anything, and no fee is recoverable, and there is no mechanism by which a fee already paid may be recovered and none is created.**

**Nothing was dissolved. CivicCore is not re-run and is not an offer to anybody. It keeps the physical network, the hinges, the floor, the board with forty-one entries on it and every contract, and it can no longer sell a place in a queue.** Rook's price for losing it, in a doorway: **a thing that was going to happen anyway now has to be argued for** — and that is the batch-level cost, and it is not CivicCore's, and it is the sentence the volume hands Volume 03. The class of node the ruling names includes a node in a building of thirty-one rented rooms, because **an empty holder field is the absence of every possible distinction and is therefore not a distinction**, and no consequence is provided for, and a managing agent has a meter round at that building on Saturday 30 August at ten in the morning and nobody in the world can tell her what she will find.

**THE BOARD FORMS. Monday 1 September, eight seats, filled by the person named in the answer to a question asked for a state and not for a list, where that person lives under the node they answer for. Nobody was appointed. Nobody was asked in advance. A person named without being asked may decline afterwards in his own words and nothing follows from it. There was one refusal, on a piece of paper in a file, by **Ronan Vasquez, 48, estates, named on a public sheet in the vestibule of the Old Ward and dated to Friday 22 August, ten days before the eight seats were filled** — he refused in his own words, he did not read section 11 and nobody has told him what is in it, and the reason is the same sentence he put on the decontamination acceptance seven days earlier, which is that it is not a service, it is a person reading a strip, and if anybody makes it a rota he will stop and they can have the paragraph number instead. **He is why the eighth seat is Gloria Nye's and Nye is seated.** Wren Court, thirty-one rooms, no civic address, was not seated; the room tried; and being right about that is now printed on a board. **Thursday 4 September, the Methodist hall on Carrow Lane, about thirty people: the ninth seat filled in eleven minutes in an aisle by a woman of fifty-four in an apron who stated the whole price out loud and hid none of it — one of her, findable since 1995, a lease and a shopfront, never found in nineteen years by anybody holding a document; unpaid until sixty-one; a person and not a body; not a veto, not a chair, and the other eight cannot remove her; and the thirty-one whose water it is are four hundred yards away and are not seated and she is not going to pretend she is the same thing as them. She is the one woman out of about forty people in that hall who anybody can write to.** The board's first act was to have the registrar's statement read out — one power, no way to lose it, nine chairs, the ninth of them is a person — and the first thing anybody said was *then what are we for*, and they spent an hour and ten minutes on it, and they are not allowed to answer it. **Nina Calder refused the fifth page, in a public room at a counter, unargued, and the reason is the volume's: a form is the last thing a person writes and the first thing an institution keeps.**

**THE SEPTEMBER RECORDS, both published, one with a name in it and one without.** The quarterly report carries a state, a date and a source under *lowland evacuation capacity: unresolved*, and the source is a woman's own pencil book kept for nineteen years at the barrier end: **nine hundred and forty marks, a count of occasions and not of persons, and the number of persons is not on the page and has never been worked out and will not be.** Raynor-Hale put his own name on it, said in advance that he had known since Wednesday, did it anyway, and told Jonas Mercer not to argue. **That is the cost, paid on purpose, and it is a woman of about sixty who is now findable in a document that goes to the Assembly.** The first two lines of that paragraph are Jonas Mercer's and he had known what the other four said since the Wednesday before and said nothing, and it took him eleven seconds to say nothing. Nora Bell said her sentence twice because nobody wrote it down the first time and refused a cost per head in the same words and read the minute before it went. **Dev Singh was in the room for two hours and Jonas Mercer did not go to him and has still not telephoned him.** The chair's promised record of the nodes with no party answering for them was delivered on 21 August and published in September: **one page, a record of shapes, no name box on it by decision and not by oversight, and it may not be matched against any list of addresses.** Jonas Mercer refused to manufacture the count a second time and it never had to be manufactured.

**THE SEPTEMBER QUARTERLY'S OTHER HALF.** At 20 to seven on the Monday morning, at the bottom of Quarry Street, a woman with a folding trolley stopped and said *you are the one who cost me the day and the seven hours*, and he said yes, and she said she knew it was him because a man came and stood over the plate in the rain and said nothing — and then told him there is no date on her and no date on the stand and there is going to be a date on any piece of paper about it and no person on it, and that is the only deal there is. She gave no name. He asked for nothing and wrote nothing down for two days.

**THE TRAY.** The 6-L/14 level-three acceptance came back signed on Wednesday 13 August 2014 by **Peter Naylor, 44, level-three acceptance officer, field services, Gantry Ward, out of estates since 2011**, in about ninety seconds, and the line is unchanged: *reported not maintainable, release recommended, not released.* **He does not know what any of the words in it mean, he is the man Nina Calder said out loud in a records room in May and has never been able to ask, and nobody has rung him and two people told Jonas Mercer not to.** A second tray has been on a shelf in that building since 12 August with a rubber band and a date in biro and one question outstanding — is there any party who may consent to the works — **and the answer is no, and it has been no since 29 May, and it was no when he signed.** The six words are Jonas Mercer's and the file does not say so, and the duty at 6-L/16 is held by the man who lodged them, and the two facts are nine lines apart in one drawer in one building and nobody is required to read both. **The volume's answer to all of it is a sentence about a man with a key and a bucket under a tap, and it is not a fix**, and it was said to a man in a CivicCore apron on a lowland at ten past ten on Tuesday morning who asked for a piece of paper and was told there is none, and who said: *that is a worse answer than the one I came for, and I have never not done it before and I have no idea what I am going to do instead, and in about four years one of us is going to have to say the sentence out loud and it will not be a registrar.*

**THE TUESDAY, paid.** Royden Achebe telephoned the shop on 14 August, as he had known since 18 May that he would, and said he was not asking Jonas Mercer to do the duty — he was asking him to turn up, keep his hands off the plate, write down the times, and have it entered twice. **Jonas Mercer is the one who answered**, on 16 September, and stood on a lowland lip for three hours and eleven minutes and did not go down the ladder, and Emeka Rusk did the work in his own hand with the operator box left blank for the twenty-seventh time since 2 April, and the entry was made twice and nobody was blamed, and Jonas Mercer's name went in a margin he initialled because there is no field for the man who watched it.

**THE LETTER, written by the wrong person, with his name at the bottom.** On Friday 19 September at ten past four a telephone rang four times and a woman of about seventy-one, who gave no name and was not asked for one, said the whole of what she wanted: **put nineteen ninety-eight on it and do not put who on it** — and then: **do not send me a copy and do not tell me that you have sent it, because a person who knows that a letter has been sent is a person somebody can ask whether the letter has been answered.** One sheet, ink, no crest, no typed copy, no number eight, no name but one, dated in a sentence, posted by hand on 22 September, asking a freeholder's agent not to install four meters in a cupboard that has never had a lock. **The wrongness, in the margin of his own draft: there is his name at the bottom of it and there is nobody else's, and the woman it is for has not asked him to write it, and nobody has asked her anything.** Calder refused to type it, because a typed letter on this office's machine is a departmental document and a departmental document is a record and a record gets asked for by the next managing agent in four years, and that agent will be looking for a list of who carries water in a block with no water in it. **The block's node is still at Tier 2 at £4,200. There is no water in that block. The letter is not going to fix a riser and says so nowhere, because a letter that says what it is not is a second letter. Ivy Marsden, 72, is not told, and has never been asked, and he has written that down twice.**

**THE VOLUME'S QUESTION, PUT TO THE ONLY TWO PEOPLE WHO HAVE ANSWERED IT, IN THE SAME WEEK.** Pell, in a shop, on his feet, with a sandwich on the bench, came to say he was finished saying the sentence about who pays for a refusal, and handed Jonas Mercer a job instead of a ruling: the day you ask somebody it will be a person in a room who can say no, and when they say no that ends the ask. Then, in a doorway: *do not telephone me about the first of October.* Then, after two minutes: *forty-one minutes, Mr Mercer. I have walked it.* In a kitchen off a landing on 24 September, she refused to hold the question and told him to answer it himself, and he answered it out loud for the first time in five months: **nobody pays, nobody is required, nobody is released — and the only instrument anybody in this city has built in five months that touches any of it is a slip on a public board that says a person may refuse to be counted, and a person who is not counted is not a person who is helped.** And: **the day after the first of October, she declined to answer it, and gave the reason, and told him not to write it down. He did not write it down. He wrote down that he was told not to write it down, and that is the first page anybody in this city has produced since March that cannot be given to anybody.**

**THE CLOSE.** Friday 26 September, the vestibule, and the register of narrowings has sixteen entries and has had sixteen since 4 August, and that is now a stated position rather than an accident. He is asked to chair the board's second sitting and refuses in a corridor with a reason, and the reason is that a chair decides which of two things gets heard first, and in about two years somebody is going to use the word *administrator* about him in a room. Pell, in a corridor, at last, says that the other eight cannot remove the ninth and a registrar can require the board to tell her what she is answerable for and cannot require her to be removed, and that the fourth page of section 11 is a dissolution clause at six vacancies, and that the one seat in that body which cannot be emptied by anybody else is the only seat that can empty it — **and he will not say the next sentence, and a clerk minuted that the next sentence was not said, and Calder's reason went under it, and nobody in the corridor added anything.** Monday 29 September, a clerk asks Yolanda Fofana how it feels, and she says **it feels like the only one who can get out**, and then gives three more minutes and asks for it to be minuted that this is not modesty. At ten past five, in a shop, **he writes two dates on a bench in ink along the grain, an inch and a half high: 1 OCT and 11 OCT**, and Bea Osunde puts the light out over them and says there is nothing in this borough that can be argued with a mark on a piece of wood that nobody is required to read — and then says the thing the volume has been refusing to say to him since April: **the names are the work, and you have not started, and I would rather you did it badly in a hurry than well in a year, and I am not going to be standing in this shop in a year saying that you were careful.**

### Numbers that changed something

**NINETY-TWO** in a ring-number box on 4 August, against 106 in May, and the difference between them is fourteen and is not a household · 122 on the rent roll and Block C's thirty rooms now publicly legible · £4,361.60 on no form · **fourteen, which is on no instrument and may not be put in the same sentence as nine hundred and forty and was not** · £4,200 a year still paid for a block with no water · £1,180 of stone and a sill beam on nobody's invoice · 6-L/14 accepted **Wednesday 13 August 2014** by **Peter Naylor, 44**, line: *reported not maintainable, release recommended, not released* · a second tray with two questions, one outstanding, on a shelf · nine seats, eight filled without an ask and one filled in a hall · six lowland water points at £1,880 and £11,280 a year, in a minute, and nobody asked · **nine hundred and forty marks, which are times and not people, and are now in a published report with their source** · one day and seven hours, unchanged, and a woman who has now said his name's not in it · one letter, one year, one name, zero households · two dates in biro on a beech bench.

### Lattice notices

**Three printed, in three chapters, at most one each. Nothing defining *holder*. The Ch 76 occupancy condition was not reprinted. The 1 March line on the enamel strip was not reissued, restated or quoted.**

- **Ch 91, the KH-4/20 plate at ankle height in a corridor in Kestrel Hill (new):** `LAST VERIFIED ESSENTIAL USE: NONE RECORDED. SERVES: THE MAINTENANCE OF THIS NODE. / THE MAINTENANCE OF THIS NODE IS THE STAIR, THE DOOR AND THE LIFT.`
- **Ch 93, a printed card on the Old Ward board beside the register of narrowings (new):** `A BODY THAT CANNOT BE REQUIRED TO DO ANYTHING CANNOT BE ASKED WHAT IT WILL DO. A BODY THAT MAY NOT BE CHALLENGED CANNOT BE TOLD TO STOP. THE TWO SENTENCES TOGETHER ARE THE REASON A REGISTER IS NOT A ROOM.`
- **Ch 96, the enamel strip under the register of narrowings (new, unsigned, undated, unattributed):** `WHERE A CONSENT MAY BE GIVEN, AND MAY BE GIVEN AGAIN, THE RECORD OF THE SECOND CONSENT IS NOT EVIDENCE THAT THE FIRST WAS WITHDRAWN, AND THE PARTY THAT TOOK THE FIRST CONSENT MAY RELY ON IT UNLESS THE RECORD SAYS OTHERWISE.` — this is the condition that names the 1 October problem without solving it, and **it is the volume's last live sentence.**

### Untouched, as required

No death, no rescue by chance, no lucky timing. **Neither Wednesday 1 October 2014 nor Saturday 11 October 2014 is crossed or dramatised anywhere in the batch.** Both appear only as dates on a wall, a strip, a pad, a margin and a bench, held by other people. No root reading, root clause, succession, Iona, Evan or Quiet Cut. No 48-minute figure anywhere. The Weir, the annex, the retainer, the burnt letter, the boy of nineteen, the map's margin, Prue Calloway's finding, the Saint Orra west rota, Dev Singh's empty-crossing clause, Margo Doré's advance refusal of a pump, the Selah Mora log, the tin, the brass tag, the blank line at the bottom of the form, and the sealed envelope in the drawer on a lower floor — **which Jonas Mercer stood six feet from on 21 August and did not open.** **The claims box closed in July and nothing was ever written into it and nothing was written into the envelope.** **The refusal-to-be-thanked beat of Chapter 90 — a person's thanks being a record about them, which is what makes them findable — was not reused. No character in Chapters 91–100 refuses gratitude in that sense, nobody thanks Ivo Bamba or Yvonne Amoah, and the two sentences in this batch that come nearest it are about credit for a thing and not about being foundable, and they are in different mouths.** Nine hundred and forty was never a number of people. Fourteen and nine hundred and forty were never in the same sentence.

### Character change

- **Jonas Mercer** learned that a refusal given in advance to a mechanism is the only instrument there is, that being the only person anybody can be given a fact to and cannot be asked for anything is both the safest place in the city and a place he cannot leave, and that **the names are the work and he has not started.** He refused a log, a chair, a count and a form in five months and wrote his own name at the bottom of a letter he was not authorised to write. He has still not telephoned Marguerite Oyelaran, and that is now permanent.
- **Yolanda Fofana** became the ninth seat, said her own price out loud in a hall, was asked how it feels and said she feels like the only one who can get out, and **declined to answer the volume's last question and told him not to write it down.**
- **Oren Pell** made his fifth ruling a door, decided it was a policy, stopped saying the sentence he has said five times, and spent the last thing he handed Jonas Mercer on protecting a woman from a true sentence.
- **Nina Calder** read the fourth page of a regulation and told nobody and gave a reason, refused the fifth page in a public room, refused to type a letter, and produced a public record with no name box on it by decision.
- **Ronan Vasquez** signed a log, declined a seat, and told a corridor in advance that he would stop in November and did not want to be asked to be careful about it.
- **Marguerite Oyelaran** won the case against her own interest, entered a number on purpose, refused her money back, and said in a room that she could not say who is going to be hurt by it.
- **Alden Rook** lost the thing he was selling, kept the thing he built, and named the price of the ruling in a doorway in under a minute.
- **Wendell Pryce** asked for a piece of paper, was refused by a document, and said the true thing about nine years and a woman of sixty with a pencil, and did not do it anyway.
- **Sanaa Iqbal** gave a patient-level fact and asked for nothing, and the reason she gave is the safest fact in the volume.
- **Henry Oduya** asked for nothing and is not on any document and the door works because of a wedge.

### Power and continuity state

**Jonas Mercer ends the volume Stage 2, unchanged in capability, and a transfer steward in practice and not in title.** No civic edit. One duty moved in May, one duty attended in September that he could not hold and did not touch, and one refusal of a mending in a cupboard in July that cost a person a day and seven hours and is still on a form with his name on it. **Every duty that moved was moved with a named consenting holder, a witness, a paid or unpaid flag, and his own name in the record, and the record is public.** He cannot move a signature, he cannot be required to do anything, he cannot require a person to be told anything, he cannot unmake a price and he cannot make a trust notice that it has been notified. **The body cost is unchanged and he has mentioned it to nobody: the seam mark runs past the elbow, the right hand does not close, and on 25 September it went at the fourth line of a page and he finished the page with his left hand.**

**The volume's instruments, all of them still unpaid:** continuity tiering and its order · deferral, not disconnection · a duty is an act and not a place, and a duty cannot be half-shared · rule 4(c) · **a determination with a deadline and no date on it is not a deadline** · **a claim discharged for the holder is discharged for the holder and not for the person who caused it, and findability is not answerability** · **there is no mechanism in this city to make the top of a chain pay and none to make a person take a duty, and those are the same sentence, and who pays for the refusal is nobody** · **a record of one thing at one time, and a person may refuse to be counted** · **where a consent may be given twice** · **a body that cannot be required to do anything cannot be asked what it will do, and a body that may not be challenged cannot be told to stop** · **a thing that was going to happen anyway now has to be argued for, and nobody can be required to argue.**

### New canon this batch

The 28 August determination and its exact wording and what it does and does not dissolve · the nine seats, who is in each, how each was seated, and the fact that the ninth cannot be removed by anybody but herself · **page four of section 11, a dissolution clause at six vacancies, read by three people and known to nobody else** · Peter Naylor and the 13 August acceptance · Ronan Vasquez and the 15 August signature · Nina Calder's rider and the fourth line in a compliance document · the 4 August return and the number ninety-two and what it cost · the fate of KH-4/20 and Henry Oduya's wedge · the letter, the year, the wrongness and the woman who was not told · the September quarterly and the chair's record of shapes · the refusal to be thanked not being reused, and two new standing refusals · and the two dates on a bench.
