## Volume 03, Batch 0001 — Chapters 101–110

### Story Progress

**The batch opens on a number on a public board and closes on a name said out loud in a room, and neither of those things is in a document, and that is the volume's whole argument in two objects.** Jonas Mercer starts Volume 03 having spent eight months refusing to be the person anybody says yes to, and having been told in a shop that the names are the work and that he had not started. He starts it by discovering that the instrument he spent March building — a register with no name box on it, a design, a promise he never made — has been used twice to find buildings, by careful people doing exactly what it was built to do. He ends it having said a woman's name out loud, once, in a Methodist hall, in nine seconds, to a woman who had spent eleven years making sure there was no person on anything, and having done it because a letter addressed to a room cannot be delivered and a mouth is the only instrument left.

**Alongside that: a contractor audits itself and names the volume's question in its own cover note and is right; a records supervisor puts a number in a document and sends it out unsigned so that four hundred other people can be held to it instead of her; a clerk of twenty-two years writes a page out longhand and walks it forty-one minutes in the wind because a reading rule is a door in a wall and she is the only person who can open it; a woman of thirty-four in a workshop refuses a form in nine seconds and nobody argues and the job does not happen and the refusal costs nobody anything; a man of forty-four signs a line in ninety seconds and cannot be asked one question about it by anybody, ever; and a board of nine loses two chairs in six weeks and does not dissolve and cannot be made to.**

**The question the volume is arguing with — *what is a service floor made of, when the people who need it are the people who cannot buy it?* — is not answered in this batch and is not refuted in this batch. Four of the answers are on the table and none of them is a person who is helped: six water points at £11,280 a year in a minute nobody can act on; a hundred and twenty-eight rooms outside the scope of the only good audit in the city; a reading rule that works for a committee and for nobody else; and a name.**

### Batch goal, midpoint and climax

- **Goal (Ch 101–102):** find out, from what a man who is not a party can see, what a trust in Kestrel Hill did on or after the first of October, and whether the register of narrowings grew — and then show the reader, in a room he is not in, what a refusal costs when it costs nothing.
- **Mid-batch (Ch 105–106):** the antagonist states the volume's question in a document and pays for it in his own staff's time, and the protagonist pays for his best idea by going up four flights to a door that was shut on him in September and being told the price of it by the woman it was for.
- **Climax (Ch 108–110):** he says the six words are his in a room, and is told in the same room that he has now made a second man findable; a seat empties and the minute records that nobody argued; and he says a name out loud, in a room, once, and the minute says a name was stated and does not contain it.
- **Close:** the volume's second close is the smallest possible room in the city, eleven feet by nine, and what is left standing is a register with nineteen correct entries and a blank line, a document four hundred people have signed, seven chairs, four people who will stop, a lowland that cannot be seated, four tonnes of stone owed to nobody, and a Sunday in January with no date written down for the Saturday night before it.

### Character change

- **Jonas Mercer learned that findability and usefulness are two different jobs, that he has done the first one, and that doing it cost a man of forty-nine who was not in the room. He then did the second thing he has been avoiding for eight months, badly, in a hurry, in nine seconds, in front of eleven people, and nobody was helped by it, including him.** He refused a seat in eleven words. He did not ring a chief officer for the third occasion. He went to a door. He said a name.
- **Yolanda Fofana named the sixth shape and would not say where it is; offered him the seventh seat with the price of refusing it stated out loud in advance; said in a room the sentence that a body of nine with one power is nine chairs; and said three things about a person who is not in the room and is not to be visited.**
- **Nina Calder's design got used against a person twice and she said so out loud, and then built the volume's best institutional object to make being used impossible: a checkable public number that goes out unsigned so that four hundred people can be held to it instead of her.**
- **Frances Tolley wrote a page out in biro, walked it forty-one minutes in the wind, refused to be asked about the fourth page four times in a year, and told him the pattern in his own behaviour before he saw it himself.**
- **Gloria Nye said the two hardest correct things in the batch, both about liability rather than blame, and one of them about a man who was not in the room.**
- **Stanley Umeh emptied a seat in six words and nobody kept him, and the body learned in public that this is the only power it has.**
- **Cynthia Akintola refused a form in nine seconds and the room carried on and the job did not, and the reader now knows the number on the card under the rubber band is a number of Wednesdays.**
- **Ivy Marsden was told, by the wrong man, that a letter exists, and told him the price of it, and saved nobody.**
- **Bernice Ofori signed her own name to a piece of paper for the first time in eleven years, on purpose, and a man said it out loud in a room, and there is now no deal at all.**

### Power and continuity state

**Jonas Mercer ends the batch Stage 2, rule mender, unchanged in capability, a transfer steward in practice and not in title, and on the register of attachment as LAST WORKING SYSTEM ADMINISTRATOR — released by no person.** No civic edit, no mending, no duty attended, no authority exercised and none claimed. He cannot move a signature, cannot be required to do anything, cannot require a person to be told anything, cannot unmake a price, cannot make a trust notice that it has been notified, **and cannot un-say a thing he has said in a room, which is new and is the batch's whole cost.** He asked one question per public room and on four occasions asked none. The body cost is unchanged and he has still mentioned it to nobody.

**The batch's instruments, all of them still unpaid:** a reading rule, which is an act and not a document, and has now left the only room it was made in, once, in one copy, on foot · a record of shapes with no name box on it, by decision · **a record of shapes with a number in it, unsigned, checkable at a counter by anybody, for nothing, with about four hundred other people's names on it and not the designer's** · a form with no box for the person who picks up a biro, which has now been used twice to find a building and is correct · a minute with no box for a name, which recorded the saying of a name without the name · **a letter with no addressee and no form on it, which cannot be filed, refused, minuted or delivered, and which cost a woman who keeps minutes an afternoon of her own Saturday and got a woman named anyway** · a duty is an act and not a place, and a duty cannot be half-shared · a record with one name in it cannot be argued with, and a record of one name in a handbag cannot be minuted, and both of those are the same discovery · findability is not answerability, and answerability has never been findability either.

### New canon this batch

What happened after 1 October and exactly what is publicly visible about it, and that nobody entitled to know knows · **the register of narrowings at nineteen entries, and entries 17, 18 and 19 verbatim** · the reading rule of 21 August, who can use it, who cannot, and that it has left the room in one copy on foot and been refused a copy by a managing agent for a correct reason · CivicCore's audit, its cover note, its exact limits, and Alden Rook's £9,000 and four hundred evenings · that the four meters did not go in, where they went, and the condition survey notice verbatim · Ivy Marsden told, and the price of the letter, and the third half · **the second public record: 51 nodes, 38 parties, thirteen shapes, unsigned, and the four, and which three of the four can be said** · seven of nine, and both vacancies, and that nobody can fill or unfill either · the six words out loud twice, and Item 4 of the minute of 21 November, and the fourth thing Gloria Nye said about a man who was not in the room · **Bernice Ofori, and the letter of 28 November, and 18:41 on 3 December** · the woman who keeps the minutes, unnamed, and what a letter is · Frances Tolley's fifth and fourth refusals · Cynthia Akintola's tin, and 986, and the fee, and the ninth seat's statement about being paid until she is sixty-one · Nina Calder's unsigned page and her reason for it.

### Untouched, as required

No death, no rescue by chance, no lucky timing, **and no rescue at all.** **Wednesday 1 October 2014 and Saturday 11 October 2014 are neither of them staged anywhere in the batch**; both appear only as a date on a register entry, a date in a letter, a date in a shop, and two dates in the grain of a bench. No root reading, no root clause, no succession, no Iona, no Evan, no counter-amendment, no second signature, no Quiet Cut, no origin of the community-watch term, no three-line brass tokens — those are Volumes 07, 09 and 15. No 48-minute figure anywhere. The Weir, the annex, the retainer, the two-refusal question and the day after, the burnt letter, the boy of nineteen, the map's margin, the blank line at the bottom of the form, the sealed envelope in the drawer on a lower floor, Margo Doré's advance refusal of a pump, Dev Singh's empty-crossing clause, the Selah Mora log, the tin, the brass tag, the Saint Orra west rota and the 11:00–13:30 gap — **all unresolved.** The claims box closed in July and nothing was ever written into it and nothing was written into the envelope. **The refusal-to-be-thanked beat of Chapter 90 is not reused anywhere: nobody in Chapters 101–110 refuses to be thanked, nobody thanks Ivo Bamba or Yvonne Amoah, no minute records that a person has not been thanked, and the ninth seat is not thanked for anything.** Nine hundred and forty was never a number of people, nine hundred and eighty-six was never a number of people, and **neither figure has ever been in the same paragraph as fourteen.**

**One Lattice notice printed in the batch, in one chapter: the freeholder's condition-survey notice on the door of the rear block, Carrow Lane, in Chapter 106. The card in the plastic sleeve on the hall wall in Chapter 108 is not a Lattice notice and is not on the Lattice; it is a piece of paper on a wall in a Methodist hall. Nothing defined *holder*. The 1 March line on the enamel strip was not reissued, restated or quoted. The two conditions on the Old Ward board stay unreconciled and the initials on the back of the enamel strip are never decided in prose.**

### Handoff

**The next phase is Volume 03, Batch 0002: Chapters 111–120, Monday 8 December 2014 onwards, with the Saint Orra west term expiring at 06:00 on Sunday 4 January 2015.** `workspace/volume-03/batch-0002/PROMPT.md` is the batch prompt. **The "VOLUME 03, BATCH 0001 — CHAPTERS 101–110" block at the end of `state/continuity.md` is the authoritative handover and it carries the corrected calendar, all nineteen register entries, the reading rule, the audit and its limits, the meters, the second public record and the four, the seats, the Chapter 0102 person and every document that chapter made, the six words out loud and the record they are in, all ten standing refusals, and the midpoint.** Batch 0002 must inherit the corrected calendar, not the Batch 0001 cards; must say **seven of nine, two chairs empty**, not eight; must carry the deferred beat from Card 0103 — Jonas naming the stand at the bottom of Quarry Street is **deferred, not cancelled**, and 0110 has paid a different version of it; and must not put Bernice Ofori in a document, send anybody down that street, or resolve the day after.

## Volume 03, Batch 0002 — Chapters 111–120

**Monday 8 December 2014 to Wednesday 28 January 2015. The batch's engine is Beatrice Osunde's sentence from 24 November, said in a shop and not said again in the last chapter because he had not done anything about it: *being findable is not the same as being useful, and you have found out the first half in eight months, and the second half is a different job, and there are three people in this borough who need the hard one.*** And the batch's hard half is about what it costs to be useful. **No rescue, no lucky timing, no death, and nobody helped.**

### What the batch did, chapter by chapter, in one line each

**0111** a kitchen off a landing, and the second close POV in Volume 03 on an unpaid person (the volume's third so far, with Chapter 0102 and Chapter 0114's Nina Calder), and a woman of fifty-four who says in a kitchen the sentence nobody in this borough has said in a room — a person who is findable is a person who can be asked, and a person who can be asked has to have an answer ready, **and she has not got one ready and is not going to get one ready in a room, and it is the same refusal as the day after and the same reason and it stands.** **0112** two lines and the word *scope* and a chief officer who asks for them to be minuted in a second building, which is the right thing and the worst thing, and a **fourteen** that is a difference and not a household. **0113** the one man in this borough who can be asked, and a refusal to go and see him, in a shop, in thirty seconds, because the only room left is somebody's kitchen and he is not entitled to one. **0114** a letter asking for a reason column, and a reason column is a sentence column, and a sentence column is a name box with the serial numbers left off, and the seventh refusal. **0115** half past four in the morning in a waiting area with four chairs, and a gate that opens at seven because two people turned up at ten o'clock the night before, and no document about it. **0116** six water points in January, a man who asks to be counted and refuses the sixth, and a body that can do nothing and writes the nothing down in the correct words. **0117** a printed slip with three boxes and a line for a name, and nine seconds written in daylight with her own name at the top of a sheet that is not a form, because she did not write on the form, and a drawer with no key. **0118** the price of a rule said out loud in a corridor, and the rule used once, and four words at a counter. **0119** eleven seconds at a counter, and a state instead of a list, and a date, and no form. **0120** the volume's question asked as a physical question, and a sentence that is not the sentence anybody expected.

### New canon this batch

**THE ANSWER TO THE HOUSING OFFICE'S LETTER AS IT WAS READ OUT ON A THURSDAY AT HALF PAST TWO, reference HC/CR/1147, in two lines, and it is true: the association is not a party to CivicCore's service agreements and its premises are not within the scope of the audit. WHAT MARGUERITE OYELARAN DID: asked for it to be minuted as a thing said in a room, so the word *scope* stands in two files in two buildings, and said the reason out loud — a thing that is wrong in one place and right in another is a thing that gets corrected in four years by whoever is left. THE PRICE: a date against her name and the word *disputes* in a borough record, and a list is the only instrument this borough has ever offered her, and she has made herself the person most likely to be asked for one. She has not produced a list and is not going to. THE SECOND PUBLIC NUMBER AND THE FOURTEEN: 106 in a ring-number box in May, 92 in August on purpose, and a difference of fourteen worked out loud in a room on 18 December and now in a housing file and a borough record, and fourteen in a housing file reads as fourteen households and is not fourteen anything, and there is no form anywhere on which anybody could write that.**

**WHO READ THE SAINT ORRA WEST HANDOVER ALOUD ON SATURDAY 3 JANUARY 2015 AT TEN O'CLOCK AT NIGHT, IN A WAITING AREA WITH FOUR CHAIRS AND A MACHINE FOR HOT WATER THAT HAS NOT WORKED SINCE AUGUST: EZRA MBEKI and TREVOR NUNN, 58, a night porter, nineteen years, not on a roster because the roster does not exist. WHAT WAS WRITTEN DOWN: nothing. There is no document, no minute, no log entry and no date, and there is not going to be one, and the reason it is not an oversight is that it is nobody's job. THE CLIPBOARD WAS NOT TOUCHED. JONAS MERCER WAS FOUR HUNDRED YARDS AWAY IN THE RAIN AND DID NOT COME, because if he is in that corridor two tired people can say he was there in about four years, and that is a rota with one name on it. THE GATE OPENED AT SEVEN BECAUSE OF TEN O'CLOCK THE NIGHT BEFORE AND FOR NO OTHER REASON. IT IS THE FIRST TIME IN THIS ACCOUNT THAT A THING HAS BEEN GOT RIGHT BY NOBODY WRITING IT DOWN, AND NOBODY IN THIS BOROUGH IS GOING TO BE TOLD THAT IT WORKED.**

**ROYDEN ACHEBE WAS NOT TOLD. Nobody told him. Jonas Mercer did not go, did not telephone, did not use the switchboard, did not go to his team, did not go into the building and did not ask anybody, and the refusal was given out loud in a shop on 24 December and it is the hardest refusal in the batch: an apology from the man who named you in a room you were not in has to be said in a room, and the only kind of room left in this borough where it could be said is somebody's kitchen, and he has not got one and is not entitled to one, and he would rather be the man who did it in a room than the man who did it in a file, because in a file it is a handle. He wrote the reason down and not the apology, and Bea Osunde told him that is what a pad is for: it is his, there is no box on it anywhere, and that is also the point. Three men, none told anything, none resolved: the man of forty-four who cannot be asked, ever; the man of forty-nine who can be asked one question tomorrow morning and is not; and a man of forty-eight who did a weekly verification for eleven weeks and stopped in October and has not been asked and has not been rung, and the autumn compliance report is out with a paragraph number on it in front of four hundred and twenty women.**

**THE READING RULE WAS USED, ONCE, ON AN UNDATED DAY IN JANUARY 2015, IN A CORRIDOR AT THE RECORDS COUNTER, IN ABOUT ELEVEN SECONDS, BY FRANCES TOLLEY, TO PETER NAYLOR, 44, WHO WAS NOT TOLD HE WAS IN IT AND DOES NOT KNOW WHAT ANY OF THE WORDS MEAN. She is not going to say whether it worked and nobody may ask her. He is not going to be told. JONAS MERCER FOUND OUT BECAUSE THE MAN SAID FOUR WORDS AT THE COUNTER ON 22 JANUARY: `I was read to.` He did not ask. And what a corridor is for: a corridor is the only place in this borough that has no minute in it, no agenda, no box, no name, no gallery and no publication, and a rule is a door, and a door in a corridor is a door that can be opened by a person on her own time for a person who turns up. THE PRICE OF THE DESIGN, said out loud for the first time: there is no way at all to say that she has not done it on a given day, and she would not want them to be able to. It established nothing, it can be argued with, and being arguable is the point.**

**A REASON COLUMN WAS ASKED FOR IN WRITING ON 2 JANUARY 2015 BY M. FURNEAUX, 61, OF THE REAR BLOCK, CARROW LANE, AND REFUSED BY NINA CALDER AT A COUNTER IN THE RIGHT ORDER IN ABOUT ELEVEN SECONDS, AND IT IS THE SEVENTH REFUSAL, AND IT STANDS. HE IS NOT ASKING FOR A NAME. HE IS ASKING FOR A SENTENCE, AND A SENTENCE COLUMN IS A NAME BOX WITH THE SERIAL NUMBERS LEFT OFF. The letter went back with nothing in it on 5 January. NINA CALDER HAS SIX REFUSALS IN HER IN NINETEEN YEARS AND DOES NOT CALL THEM THAT AND HAS NOW WRITTEN THE LIST OUT AND CROSSED IT AND PUT UNDER IT: *seven. Stop counting.***

**ABU BAKAYOKO, 58, TWENTY-SIX YEARS AT A STAND AT THE TOP OF THE LOWLAND MARKET, ASKED TO BE COUNTED IN A ROOM IN DAYLIGHT ON 13 JANUARY 2015, and what he wanted was not a seat and not money and not a number but a line in a minute saying that a thing exists at a place where a thing is needed. HE GAVE THE STATE OF FIVE OF THE SIX IN ABOUT TWO MINUTES AND REFUSED THE SIXTH OUT LOUD BEFORE ANYBODY ASKED HIM, and asked for the refusal to be written down, and the reason is the volume's method in one sentence: there is somebody on that street who has told the county that nothing is there and she is right to and he is not going to be the person who takes it off her. NOBODY ASKED HIM. NO SEVENTH SHAPE WAS ADDED TO ANY RECORD, and the reason was given out loud in seventeen words — a shape has to have a place you can point at, and that has a person in it — and the seventh is not resolved. The minute records that this body has done nothing, in the correct words, and that the doing of nothing is not a defect in the minute. The six remain at £11,280, nobody has been asked to pay any part of it, and a district that cannot pay has a seat and a person in it and has not thereby lost its vote, and THE REASON THE LOWLAND HAS NO SEAT IS NOT THAT IT CANNOT PAY.**

**BERNICE OFORI CAME TO A HALL ON 21 JANUARY 2015 IN DAYLIGHT, ALONE, UNESCORTED, UNFOUND, AND DID NOT WRITE ON THE PRINTED SLIP FROM COMMITTEE SERVICES — a sheet with a crest, a reference and a form number is a document and nobody asked her for a document — AND WROTE NINE SECONDS AT THE TOP OF THE SECOND SHEET, WHICH IS UNSIGNED AND HAS NO BOX AND CANNOT BE USED TO SHOW ANYBODY ANYTHING, WITH HER NAME AND THE STREET AND THE DATE, AND LEFT, TAKING NOTHING WITH HER, because her own November letter was still in a handbag in a kitchen four hundred yards away. THAT SHEET — FOUR LINES OF THE MINUTE-TAKER'S BIRO WITH HER OWN FOUR ABOVE THEM — IS IN A PLASTIC WALLET IN THE DRAWER OF A TRESTLE TABLE AND THERE IS NO KEY AND THERE IS NO LIST OF WHAT IS IN ANY DRAWER IN THAT BUILDING, AND THE PRINTED SLIP IS BACK IN ITS TRAY. SHE WAS NOT TOLD OF ANYTHING, WAS ASKED FOR NOTHING, WAS GIVEN NOTHING, NOBODY WENT AND GOT HER, AND HER NAME IS NOT ON THE FORM AND MAY NOT BE PUT ON IT.**

**AND THE SENTENCE, asked on 28 January 2015 in a room in daylight by a man who is not on the agenda either, and it is a sentence and not a scheme and it is not the sentence anybody expected: A COUNTER. A PERSON WHO IS AT IT. A STATE ASKED FOR INSTEAD OF A LIST. A DATE. AND A BLANK LINE AT THE BOTTOM OF A BOARD THAT NOBODY IS REQUIRED TO WRITE ON. It came from the third of April, when two people said no in their own words and a gate went up at six nineteen in the morning anyway, and the only sentences that survive being written down are the small ones with a date on them.**

**AND WHAT IS ON THE FLOOR, which is a room and not an institution: a counter, a slot for post that has been empty since 18 July, a board with nineteen entries and a blank line, two conditions with opposite provenance, and about nine people a day.**

**AND THE SERVICE, which took eleven seconds and is the cheapest thing anybody in this borough has produced this year: a state asked for instead of a list, given by a counter that is required to answer and is not required to make one, with a date written at the top of it, and it cannot be argued with because there is nothing in it to argue with, and it will be argued with, and any person may come back to that counter on any morning and be right and get a second date.**

**PRUE CALLOWAY'S FINDING OF 23 APRIL WAS OPENED ONCE, IN A ROOM, IN DAYLIGHT, ON 28 JANUARY 2015: *a building has an address, it is the people who have no location of record, and a panel cannot weigh a claim from a person it cannot locate.* IT IS NOT RESOLVED AND IT MAY NOT BE OPENED A SECOND TIME. He said he is not going to say what he thinks it means, and that there is a fourth page of a regulation from 2008 that three people in this borough have read and told nobody, **and that he is not one of the three and is not going to say who the three are** (Frances Tolley, Oren Pell, Nina Calder; he has never read it), and he is not going to say what is on it in a room with nine people in it, and he asked for that to be minuted as a decision and not as a virtue. THE NINTH SEAT WAS IN THE ROOM AND WAS NOT TOLD ANYTHING.**

**AND A CORRECTION CARRIED OUT OF BATCH 0001: THERE IS NO CHAIR OF THAT BODY AND THERE NEVER WAS. A body of nine seats has one power and a chair is not one of the three parts of it. The ninth seat has gone through the order of things since 4 July because she is the only one of the nine anybody can write to, and that is not a chair and she has said so in a room. Any later batch that gives that body a chair is wrong.**

### Untouched, as required

No death, no rescue by chance, no lucky timing, **and no rescue at all.** No root reading, no root clause, no succession, no Iona, no Evan, no counter-amendment, no second signature, no Quiet Cut, no origin of the community-watch term, no three-line brass tokens — those are Volumes 07, 09 and 15. **ONE LATTICE NOTICE IN THE BATCH, in one chapter: the Tier 1 continuity-schedule line for six nodes, £1,880 per node per year, £11,280 per year, no party answering, term to 06:00 Sunday 7 June 2015, and it may not be matched against any list of addresses — in Chapter 0116, on the wall above the records counter. The two conditions on the Old Ward board stay unreconciled and the initials on the back of the enamel strip are never decided in prose.**

**TEN STANDING REFUSALS PLUS AN ELEVENTH, NONE ARGUED OUT, NONE SOFTENED, NONE REVISITED. AND THE STATE OF THE CITY AT THE END OF CHAPTER 120 IS: register of narrowings at NINETEEN, blank line blank, unmoved for sixty-three days; the board at SEVEN OF NINE with two chairs empty and no chair at all; the second public record out, unsigned, with about four hundred other people's names on it; the reading rule used once in a corridor by mistake and by design; the Saint Orra west gate running on a local term renewed by two people at ten o'clock at night on a Saturday nobody has written down, **a term that is six days, read on the Saturday night before the Sunday it starts on, so the Saturday nights are a week apart — 31 January, 7 February, 14 February, 21 February, 28 February, 7 March, the seventh of them in six weeks, and no date on any of them. NEVER CALL THE TERM SEVEN DAYS: it is six days and the Saturday nights are what are a week apart**; six lowland water points at a winter price that is one boiler each; the sixth shape unsaid; a name in eleven people, two pieces of paper that are not documents, and a drawer with no key; and the winter with one Sunday in it.**

### Handoff

**The next phase is Volume 03, Batch 0003: Chapters 121–130, Monday 2 February 2015 onwards, with the Saint Orra west term in force expiring at 06:00 on Sunday 1 February 2015 and the next handover on Saturday 31 January at ten o'clock at night.** `workspace/volume-03/batch-0003/PROMPT.md` is the batch prompt. **The "VOLUME 03, BATCH 0002 — CHAPTERS 111–120" block at the end of `state/continuity.md` is the authoritative handover and it carries the corrected calendar, the answer to the housing office's letter and the fourteen, the state of the Saint Orra west term and who read it and what was not written down, whether Achebe was ever told, whether the reading rule was used, the seventh refusal and the reason column, the standing of the six lowland water points and why there is no seventh shape, the two pieces of paper and the drawer with no key, the volume's sentence and what is on the floor, the opening of Prue Calloway's finding, all eleven refusals, and the correction that the board has no chair.** Batch 0003 must inherit the corrected calendar, not the Batch 0002 chapter headers' own arithmetic; must say **seven of nine, two chairs empty, and no chair of that body at all**; must not resolve the day after, must not resolve the seventh shape, must not open Prue Calloway's finding a second time, must not put Bernice Ofori in any document or send anybody down that street, and must not let any Weir resident be described in a document that leaves the street.

## Volume 03, Batch 0003 — Chapters 121–130

**TEN CHAPTERS, 2 FEBRUARY TO 13 MARCH 2015. THE BATCH'S ENGINE IS THE PRICE LIST OF AN ELEVEN-SECOND SERVICE, AND THE PRICE IS FINDABILITY. THE BATCH'S CENTRAL FINDING IS A DRIFT: a state given on a Tuesday can be wrong on the Wednesday, and a counter that is required to answer and is not required to make one will go on answering the same state for as long as nobody comes back to argue it, and there is no obligation in this city to come back, and a person turning up is not an instrument, it is a coincidence, and a coincidence every week is a service. NOBODY WAS HURT BY ANYTHING IN THIS BATCH AND NOBODY WAS HELPED BY ANYTHING IN IT, AND THAT IS STATED OUT LOUD IN CHAPTER 0122 AND NOT APOLOGISED FOR.**

**THE BATCH'S ANSWER TO ITS OWN QUESTION, given out loud in a room in daylight on Friday 13 March by a man who is not on the agenda either, in a public minute with no names in it, and it is not the five items again and it is not a scheme: A SERVICE FLOOR COSTS THE PEOPLE WHO ARE ON IT AND NOT THE PEOPLE WHO USE IT. THE PRICE IS FINDABILITY. EVERY PERSON IN THIS BOROUGH WHO HAS EVER STOOD AT THAT COUNTER IS NOW SLIGHTLY EASURE TO FIND THAN THEY WERE, INCLUDING A WOMAN OF FIFTY-FOUR WHO HAS STOOD IN A SHOPFRONT SINCE THE FOURTH OF JULY, AND NONE OF THEM WAS ASKED, AND NOBODY HAS BEEN ASKED WHETHER THEY CAN AFFORD THAT, AND THIS BOROUGH HAS NO INSTRUMENT FOR ASKING, AND THE ONLY INSTRUMENT IT HAS EVER HAD IS A FORM.**

**THE SHELF IS NINE SLIPS IN A DRAWER UNDER THE GROUND-FLOOR COUNTER, IN ONE HAND, DATED, UNSIGNED, WITH NO REFERENCE, NO NUMBER, NO CREST, NO FORM, NO BOX AND NO NAME ON ANY OF THEM INCLUDING NINA CALDER'S. She is not on a rota and cannot be made to be on one and nobody told her to. Nobody has asked her how many there are and she is not going to tell and the back of her hand is not a book. **NINE SLIPS AND ABOUT ELEVEN PEOPLE A DAY ARE TWO DIFFERENT NUMBERS ABOUT TWO DIFFERENT THINGS, THEY ARE NOT A CONTRADICTION, AND THEY MUST NEVER BE ADDED TOGETHER: a slip is only written when a person stands in front of her and asks what was true on a day, and out of nine people a day, and then eleven, the ones who ask that are three men who have all come more than once and one other person about whom nobody has said a word, and that is the whole of them. A form that added the two would say *this counter sees eleven people and produces one document*, and that is a level of service, and there is no level of service in that room. SHE GAVE NEITHER NUMBER TO ANYBODY, INCLUDING IN THE ONE ANSWER SHE GAVE A STRANGER ABOUT HERSELF ON 6 MARCH, and the count of nine is the count of this account and not the count of a person.** There is no form in this borough on which anybody could write that they are not a series, and there is not going to be one. The interval that matters is 2 February to 13 February: eleven days, and eleven days has no word, and a series has a word for the gap. THE THROUGHPUT IS ABOUT ELEVEN A DAY WHERE IT WAS ABOUT NINE, and a number a person can be held to is a level of service and the day there is a level of service somebody can cut it.**

**THE THREE ASKERS AND WHAT THEY WERE GIVEN. ABU BAKAYOKO, 58, with a bowser, came back twice, on 2 February and 13 February, and was right to and may not be made un-right; the first answer was that what she can be held to is a state on a date and a date is a thing about a time and a trough is a place; the second was the first thing anybody in this borough has offered anybody in about four months, and it is worth nothing and it works — a second date, on a second slip, saying what was true on that date, signed by nobody, kept by a person in a vestibule eleven feet by nine — and the sentence it produced is that a man who can be wrong in public is not a man who has to be right in a van. EMMANUEL OTIENO, 38, agency worker, nights at a depot, a daughter of nine who walks to a bus stop, not on any list, was given a state and a date about a tap on a yard wall and refused in eleven seconds about a bus stop, and was not angry, and said eleven words on the way out the door: A PERSON CAN ASK A QUESTION HERE AND GET A DATE — and then worked out alone, in a kitchen, on a Sunday, that the difference is not the answer but that a date can be wrong and if it is wrong you can come back, and that he can now do this to anything, and that is the danger. HOWARD BRAMBLE, 61, of a block of forty flats in Tideglass, asked for a reason column, was refused in the right order in about eleven seconds, said out loud in a room in daylight IT IS NOT A LIST, and had the best argument anybody has made in this borough since the first of March: you will give me a date and you will not give me a reason, and both are the same shape, and the only reason the counter can do one and not the other is that the first is about a thing and the second is about a person. A fourth ask, from a man of fifty about his sister, was refused in eleven seconds and correctly, and he went out into the rain with nothing, and the asking was recorded by nobody. And the only thing anybody in this borough has ever said that was both free and a use was given to that stranger, in about eleven seconds, and it was about a drawer, and it was: THE COUNTER WORKS BECAUSE A PERSON IS ON IT, AND SHE IS NOT FINDABLE, AND IF SHE STOPPED COMING DOWN THERE WOULD BE A COUNTER AND THERE WOULD NOT BE A COUNTER.**

**THE SAINT ORRA WEST TERM, AFTER 1 FEBRUARY AND AFTER 1 MARCH. A term is six days, is read at 22:00 on the Saturday night before the Sunday it starts on, and the gate opens at 07:00 on that Sunday and the term expires at 06:00 on the following Sunday. The Saturday nights are a week apart. NEVER CALL THE TERM SEVEN DAYS AND NEVER CALL IT A WEEK. The chain: 24 Jan, 31 Jan, 7, 14, 21, 28 Feb, 7 and 14 March 2015, and there is no date written down anywhere in this city for a single one of them and there is not going to be one, because a person is not required to be told and there is nobody in this borough who is required to write it. WHO READ THEM ALOUD, on every one since 3 January: EZRA MBEKI, in and out of that building since 1992, and TREVOR NUNN, 58, a night porter, nineteen years, who is not on a roster because the roster does not exist. Neither is on any instrument. Neither knew until about nine o'clock. It took about nine minutes. NOTHING WAS WRITTEN DOWN. Jonas Mercer has never been told that any of them occurred, has never asked and is not going to ask. HE HAD BEEN IN THAT CORRIDOR ONCE, at half past four in the morning on Sunday 4 January 2015. HE DID NOT GO ON 1 FEBRUARY AND DID NOT DECIDE NOT TO GO, and there was nothing on any day of that week that said a man had chosen anything — THAT WAS THE FOURTH ONE, AND THAT IS WHAT A FOURTH ONE IS, AND A FOURTH IS A HABIT AND A HABIT IS A ROTA WITH ONE NAME IN IT. On Monday 2 March, in daylight, in a ground-floor corridor, on foot, off the street, he told BRAM OSEI out loud, on purpose, that he was not going to be in that room on Saturday 7 March, and Bram Osei said he would not say that he was there and would not say that he was not there, and has told nobody, and it cost nothing, and it is the only thing in nine months Jonas Mercer has got right by not doing it. THE FINDING: THE MAN WAS NEVER IN IT. THE GATE HAS NEVER ONCE BEEN OPENED BY ANYTHING HE DID IN A CORRIDOR AT HALF PAST FOUR IN THE MORNING. A counter in this borough was asked nothing about the gate and looked for nothing about the gate, and the reason given out loud is that a counter with an opinion about a gate is a service. The rota has still not been rebuilt and cannot be; the 11:00–13:30 row is still empty and does not say *vacant*; Bram Osei's Thursday is still refused in his own handwriting; Priya Ranganathan's card is still on the Friday and Saturday rows because she put it there.**

**THE COMPLIANCE PARAGRAPH WAS NEVER ANSWERED, BY NOBODY, AND IS NOT GOING TO BE. Paragraph 4.11, decontamination, west site, level zero; the historical half of the weekly verification record cannot be reconstructed and the non-conformance is carried forward; there is no name in it because there has never been a name field. The log is a supplier's grid of fifty-two Sundays with a tick in a temperature box for four books and a signature box empty on every line, and it has carried no name since 9 May. Four hundred and twenty women have instruments out of that room every weekday. THE MAN OF FORTY-EIGHT WAS NOT TOLD ANYTHING, WAS NOT ASKED AND WAS NOT RUNG, and Jonas Mercer said the reason out loud, once, in a corridor, to a porter: BECAUSE IF I TELL HIM THEN HE IS THE PERSON WHO KNOWS, AND A PERSON WHO KNOWS IS A PERSON SOMEBODY CAN ASK, AND THE ONLY INSTRUMENT IN THIS BOROUGH THAT KEEPS A MAN FROM BEING ASKABLE IS A MAN WHO DOES NOT KNOW. HE IS SAFER NOT KNOWING. It is the first time in this account that the correct action has been to leave a person in ignorance on purpose, and Bram Osei said it is also true about him. What it cost: nothing measurable, and that is the finding. The machine is still running; its own strip goes in a drawer in the room; a strip is a piece of paper and a drawer is a place and a piece of paper does not put itself in a drawer, so somebody has been putting strips in that drawer about forty times a day, and that person HAS NOT BEEN FOUND EITHER, and Jonas Mercer is not going to look. ONE LATTICE NOTICE IN THE BATCH, in Chapter 0122, at Saint Orra west. Nothing defines *holder*. The 1 March line is not reissued, restated or quoted. The two conditions on the Old Ward board stay unreconciled and the initials on the back of the enamel strip are never decided in prose.**

**ROYDEN ACHEBE, 49, WAS NEVER TOLD, BY NOBODY, IN FEBRUARY OR AT ANY POINT IN THIS BATCH. Jonas Mercer did not go to Gantry Ward, did not telephone, did not use the switchboard, did not go to the team, did not go to the building, did not ask whether he was in and did not stand on a landing. What changed in February is the price: a counter in this borough now mints findability, and findability used to cost a day and a raincoat and a decision, and a date is the cheapest handle in this city. A date about a trough costs eleven seconds and a slip and is worth exactly that; a date about him saying a man's name in a room on 21 November would cost about the same, and that is the problem, and it took him four days to write it down. BEATRICE OSUNDE, in the shop: A RECEIPT IS NOT AN APOLOGY, AND THE WHOLE OF WHAT HE HAS BEEN PROTECTING THAT MAN FROM FOR EIGHT MONTHS IS A BILL. And the sentence the batch ends on: **YOU HAVE BEEN FINDING PEOPLE FOR EIGHT MONTHS AND YOU HAVE NOT YET FOUND OUT THAT THE EXPENSIVE HALF IS NOT BEING FOUND. IT IS BEING ASKED.** FINDABILITY AND ASKABILITY ARE TWO DIFFERENT CURRENCIES AND THIS BOROUGH HAS BEEN MINTING THE FIRST ONE VERY SUCCESSFULLY SINCE THE THIRD OF APRIL AND HAS NOT SPENT ONE UNIT OF THE SECOND ONE AT ALL. And PETER NAYLOR, 44, a wall, cannot be asked anything, ever, and has a fortnight somewhere in this city with a bucket under a tap that came round at the end of February in four degrees with a frost warning out, and nobody is able to ask him whether he has got a coat, and nobody helped him.**

**THE REGISTER OF NARROWINGS DID NOT MOVE ONCE IN THIS BATCH. NINETEEN ENTRIES, BLANK LINE BLANK, AND ON FRIDAY 13 MARCH 2015 THE LINE HAD BEEN BLANK FOR A HUNDRED AND SEVEN DAYS, THE LONGEST IT HAS EVER BEEN STILL SINCE THE FOURTH OF AUGUST. Entry 19 is a bulb and the bulb is out. The slot for post, empty since 18 July, HAS HAD A CARD IN IT SINCE 20 FEBRUARY, four inches by two, folded once, not a form, no crest, no reference, no box, no envelope, about ninety people have seen it, NOBODY HAS TAKEN IT OUT AND NOBODY HAS PUT IT UP, and Nina Calder has not read it and is not going to, because a slot that gets emptied is a rule and a rule is a service and hers is the name it would have. What is on the card is not known to anybody in this account and may not be stated. And the two conditions sixteen inches apart are still unreconciled, and the batch's finding about them is physical and not moral: A PERSON WALKING UNDER TWO CONDITIONS THAT DO NOT AGREE GOES FASTER. A CONTRADICTION IS NOT A PAUSE, IT IS A FLOOR THAT IS NOT LEVEL, AND EVERYBODY CROSSES A FLOOR THAT IS NOT LEVEL FASTER THAN A FLOOR THAT IS. And the corollary: THE FLOOR OF A SERVICE IS A PLACE WHERE A PERSON CAN STAND UNDER A CONTRADICTION AND BE ANSWERED. It is eleven feet by nine and it is not going on a wall. And the most dangerous sentence anybody in this borough could have said in February was *that is the rules*, nobody said it, one of the two would have been right if they had, and Nina Calder refused out loud, unasked, to say which one and would rather stand under a contradiction for another four years than be the woman who settled it.**

**THE REASON COLUMN WAS REFUSED TWICE AND IT IS NOT A LIST, AND THE WORDS WERE SAID OUT LOUD IN A ROOM IN DAYLIGHT IN FEBRUARY AND SOMEBODY WROTE THEM DOWN ON THE BACK OF HIS HAND. M. FURREAUX, 61, rear block, Carrow Lane, wrote once on 2 January, was refused, and is not going to write again before about 2018. HOWARD BRAMBLE, 61, of a block of forty flats in Tideglass, asked in person on 16 February and was refused in about eleven seconds in the right order, and said afterwards that the refusal was right and that in about four years there is going to be nothing at all to put on the table for a frightened person in a kitchen. THE TWO MEN ARE NOT TO BE CONFUSED AND THE COINCIDENCE OF THEIR AGE IS NOT A PATTERN. NINA CALDER'S STANDING COUNT OF REFUSALS IS SEVEN AND SHE DID NOT ADD TO IT. The finding: A REQUEST CAN BE REFUSED TWICE AND THE SECOND REFUSAL IS CHEAPER, NOT EASIER, BECAUSE BY THE SECOND TIME BOTH OF THEM KNOW WHAT IT IS FOR, and it cost her about nine minutes instead of eleven seconds, and nobody is going to be able to argue a refusal out of her by getting better at it. And her price, given out loud: if the reason is going to exist anywhere it is going to exist on a slip from a counter with a date on it and nobody's name on it, about a thing and not about a decision, and that is a very poor document and it will settle nothing, and she would rather it did not exist at all.**

**A LETTER CAME INTO THE RECORDS OFFICE ON WEDNESDAY 18 FEBRUARY 2015 AT ABOUT HALF PAST NINE, not on borough stock, with a real departmental reference in the correct typeface, about four lines long. It is not a reason column. It is the smallest request anybody in this borough has made since the first of March, and its contents are not stated in this account and may not be stated in a room or opened in a document. NINA CALDER HAS NOT OPENED IT IN FRONT OF A SECOND PERSON, HAS TOLD NOBODY IT IS THERE, HAS NOT MOVED IT, AND HAS NOT ANSWERED IT IN TWENTY-THREE DAYS AS AT FRIDAY 13 MARCH (nineteen days as at Monday 9 March, and the count is exact). This is the live hook of the batch. Nobody is going to be helped by it and nobody has been harmed by it.**

**ELEVEN STANDING REFUSALS, THE MAN OF FORTY-EIGHT BEING THE ELEVENTH AND NOT A TWELFTH, NONE ARGUED OUT, NONE SOFTENED, NONE REVISITED, AND A REQUEST REFUSED TWICE IS ONE REQUEST AND NOT TWO SO THE REASON COLUMN REFUSALS OF 2 JANUARY AND 16 FEBRUARY DO NOT MAKE THE TOTAL TWELVE. The mortuary porter's four words in nine seconds · Marguerite Oyelaran's nine seconds and a person-box, and the difference of fourteen is a difference between two public numbers and is on no instrument and is not fourteen households · Emeka Rusk's, and helping does not go on a form · Margo Doré's advance refusal of a pump, and nobody went up the Weir path in this batch · Sanaa Iqbal's twenty minutes a week, and the compliance paragraph behind it is unanswered and unanswered is the standing state · Ivo Bamba's, spent in Chapter 90, not reused · Nina Calder's fifth page and the seventh of 2 January, and a second refusal of the same request on 16 February which is NOT the eighth because it is not a list · Yolanda Fofana's refusal to answer the day after, and Tomas Renn's question is still unanswered and is still the engine and Batch 0004 may not resolve it or let anybody manufacture it · Frances Tolley's, in a corridor, on 26 November, and a fifth on 22 January, the rule used once and not used again in this batch and not to be used again in Volume 03 · Bernice Ofori's, and a second on 21 January, in nine seconds, in her own hand, on a sheet that is not a form, untouched and unreferenced in this batch and nobody went to the bottom of Quarry Street · and a man of forty-eight, in a corridor in August, about a job in November having a department in it, whom nobody has asked and whom Jonas Mercer has now refused out loud to go and find.**

**NOBODY REFUSES TO BE THANKED ANYWHERE IN VOLUME 03. No minute records that a person has not been thanked. The ninth seat is not thanked for anything. The refusal-to-be-thanked beat is spent and did not fire once in Batch 0003; the one place a character came close to it was cut.**

**THE STATE OF THE CITY AT THE END OF CHAPTER 130, Friday 13 March 2015: the register of narrowings at nineteen and the blank line blank for a hundred and seven days and the post slot holding a card nobody removes; the board at SEVEN OF NINE, two chairs, and NO CHAIR OF THAT BODY AND THERE NEVER WAS, a chair not being one of the three parts of its one power, which are to require a party holding continuity to answer, receive the answer, and be asked to consider a matter; the September record public, the determination public, the quarterly public with a number of times and its source in the same paragraph; the second public record out and unsigned with no name box and about four hundred other people's names on it, eleven extracts taken, two wrong write-backs, and *enclosure received, no comment* in the file; the reading rule in one woman's inside pocket, used once in a corridor on a man of forty-four who does not know, and nobody may ask her whether it worked and nobody may ask him anything, ever; the letter worked and its price a sheet of A4 on a door and a woman more frightened; six words his and two men findable because he said things in rooms, one a wall and one a door, neither told anything; a woman's name in about eleven people and in no document, in a drawer with no key and in a handbag; six lowland water points at a winter price that is one boiler each and nobody asked to pay any part of it, and no seventh shape added to any record; a paragraph number in front of four hundred and twenty women; nine slips in a drawer and a card in a slot and a letter in a tray; and the winter's one Sunday in every week, between six and seven, when no term is in force at all, with no date written down for any of them, and a handover on Saturday 14 March 2015 at ten o'clock at night, the eighth of them in seven weeks, that nobody has written down and nobody will.**

**Prue Calloway's finding of 23 April 2014 was opened once, in a room, in daylight, on 28 January 2015, and it is not resolved, and IT MAY NOT BE OPENED A SECOND TIME. What is left of it is its consequence, and nobody in this borough has been made to live under the consequence yet and nobody is going to be. The claim to open a door in this volume is spent. Volume 03 is not closed by Chapter 130 and remains at Chapters 101–144, with Batch 0004 to follow.**

### Handoff

**The next phase is Volume 03, Batch 0004: Chapters 131–140, SATURDAY 14 MARCH 2015 TO WEDNESDAY 29 APRIL 2015 (the calendar was extended after review so that all ten cards fall inside it), opening with a Saint Orra west handover at 22:00 on Saturday 14 March — the eighth of them in seven weeks, and no date on it — which is staged once, in Chapter 0131.** `workspace/volume-03/batch-0004/PROMPT.md` is the batch prompt. **The "VOLUME 03, BATCH 0003 — CHAPTERS 121–130" block at the end of `state/continuity.md` is the authoritative handover** and it carries the drift and the shelf, the three askers and what they were given and what it was worth, the compliance paragraph and the paragraph number, the state of Royden Achebe and Peter Naylor and the man of forty-eight, the whole Saint Orra west chain and the fifth refusal and the fact that Jonas Mercer said it out loud to a porter, the two conditions and the finding about the floor that is not level, the two new open objects (the card in the post slot and the letter of 18 February), the register and the hundred and seven days, what it costs and who said it, all eleven standing refusals, the corrected seven-of-nine board state with two empty chairs and no chair of that body at all, the minor figures introduced in this batch, and the list of what may not be resolved. **Batch 0004 must never add the nine slips to the about eleven people a day, and must never let any character say either number.** Batch 0004 must not resolve the day after, must not resolve the seventh shape, must not open Prue Calloway's finding, must not put Bernice Ofori in any document or send anybody down that street, must not let any Weir resident be described in a document that leaves the street, must not use the reading rule again, must not let a counter answer anything about a gate, and must not make a number of about eleven a day into a capacity.

## Volume 03, Batch 0004 — Chapters 131–140

**Window: Saturday 14 March 2015 to Wednesday 29 April 2015. Ten chapters. The series is at 140 of 720. VOLUME 03 IS OPEN AND RUNS TO CHAPTER 144, AND CHAPTER 0140 IS NOT THE VOLUME'S CLOSE AND SAYS SO. Chapters 141–144 are the next phase and have not been written.**

**THE ONE SENTENCE THIS BATCH PRODUCED, GIVEN OUT LOUD IN A VESTIBULE ELEVEN FEET BY NINE, IN DAYLIGHT, ON MONDAY 27 APRIL 2015, TO A MAN OF ABOUT FIFTY-SIX WHO IS NOT ON THE AGENDA EITHER, IN A PUBLIC MINUTE WITH NO NAMES IN IT, ITEM 4, AND NOTHING IN THE ITEM REQUIRES ANY PERSON TO DO ANYTHING:**

> **IT IS FOR THE PEOPLE THE BOROUGH CANNOT COUNT.**

**AND THE BATCH'S ENGINE WAS THE SECOND MOUTH OF THE THIRTEENTH OF MARCH, AND IT IS THIS: FINDABILITY IS NOT A THING THIS BOROUGH HAS BEEN MINTING. FINDABILITY IS WHAT A PRICE DOES WHEN THERE IS NO COUNTER IN FRONT OF IT. A counter is the cheapest privacy device anybody in this city has ever built and it costs nothing and nobody designed it and there is no form for it. The man who turns the bucket on the board of forty slots at a bus turning head has been the most findable man in the lowland for twenty-six years and nobody did it, and he is in paragraph 6.1 of the minute of 19 May, and nobody has to spend eleven seconds to find him because the price already did it. AND ON 24 APRIL IT CAME BACK AT JONAS MERCER IN HIS OWN PERSON, in a vestibule, in daylight, when a woman of about thirty asked a records counter whether the man who stands at the wall is on a list, and the counter was right, and the answer cost about four seconds, and he stood there for about nine minutes and said nothing and there was no point at which he could have said anything. REFUSING DOES NOT MAKE THE PERSON SAFER. IT MAKES THEM UNATTACKED. The only instrument in this borough that works does not protect anybody; it only means nobody is aiming. And that is why he did not go to the bus turning head: THE FINDER IS THE HANDLE, and the cheapest handle in this city is not a date, it is a person walking up to a pillar and being handed a bucket, and the most expensive thing anybody can do to a man who already has one is to go and introduce himself.**

**THE DRIFT, AND IT IS NO LONGER A HYPOTHESIS. A STATE GIVEN ON A DAY IS A STATE ABOUT THE PERSON WHO GAVE IT. There is nobody in this city who can require a records supervisor to say what a thing is, so the only thing anybody can be handed is what a man saw from where he was standing at ten past ten on a Tuesday, and that is a true sentence, and it is in a hand, and it has a date on it, and it is wrong. THE FIRST VISIBLE ARTEFACT COST NOBODY ANYTHING: on 16 March a man of about sixty with a galvanised can asked what was in the trough by the lock-ups on the second of February and was given the state in about eleven seconds in three halves, and the state was right about the second of February and wrong about the trough, and the wrongness is not a fault of the counter, it is a fact about a Tuesday, and he went out and did not put the drum in and nobody was hurt and nobody was helped. THE SECOND KIND IS WORSE: a stale state belongs to a THING and a thing can be looked at; a contradicted state belongs to a PERSON and cannot be looked at, because only the man who said it can contradict it, and he has not come back and nobody can ask him to, and a person may not be required to answer anything including a correction. ONE HAS AN OWNER AND THE OTHER HAS A PERSON, AND THIS BOROUGH HAS NO INSTRUMENT FOR EITHER. And then the inside of it, in Chapter 0138: a state given on a day stops being a state and becomes an object in a coat, and cannot be paid, cannot be returned, cannot be filed and cannot be thrown away, because there is no procedure on the sheet — so it goes in a pocket, and nobody comes back to argue it, and going back is a decision, and nobody is required to decide anything. THAT IS THE DRIFT, AND IT IS NOT A THING THE COUNTER DOES. It is a thing that happens to a person who is handed a correct answer and given nothing to do with it. NOBODY CAME BACK TO ARGUE A STATE: Abu Bakayoko's first slip of 26 January is seven weeks old in an inside pocket and has not been looked at since, he came on 2 and 13 February and has not come since, and if he comes on a Friday for another four years that is not a man, that is a rota with one name on it that nobody wrote down and that nobody could write down. **NO INSTRUMENT WAS BUILT FOR THE DRIFT AND NO FORM WAS WRITTEN, AND THE MOMENT A FORM IS WRITTEN ABOUT ANY OF THIS THE VOLUME HAS LOST THE THING IT SPENT TWO VOLUMES BUILDING.**

**WHAT A DISCOVERY DID WHEN IT STOPPED BEING A DISCOVERY, AND IT IS THE FIRST TIME IN NINE MONTHS ANYTHING HAS COME OUT OF THAT COUNTER AND TOUCHED A DISTRICT. EMMANUEL OTIENO, 38, gave the method away three times and told nobody and wrote nothing down: a woman of about thirty-one at the depot, a man of about fifty-five on the second floor of his building, and a man about thirty he met once in a queue outside a hospital who had written a date on the back of his hand. It did not travel upwards. It went to a woman on a shift, and a man on a second floor, and a man in a queue, and from a woman on a shift to a girl on a till, and from a girl on a till to a kitchen on the other side of this borough, and every step of that is a person telling a person at a kettle and not one of the steps is a document. A man of thirty-four in a canteen said the whole of it against a wall: `It's not strange any more, that's all I wanted.` AND A DISCOVERY HAS BECOME WEATHER. What Jonas Mercer had been afraid of since 4 September was a form and a form is not going to happen. One of the three works somewhere with a name on it — a name on the drawing layer with a filled square beside it and an expiry and a node number, because eight of the nine shapes on that layer have names and the ninth has not got one and never will — and he does not know which one and is not going to find out, and asking is how a discovery becomes a finding and a finding goes in a report and a report is a thing a person can be held to. HE ASKED ONE QUESTION IN THE WHOLE BATCH, six words, in a corridor, and a corridor is a corridor and he knew that when he asked it: `And who else has got it.` AND HE IS NOT GOING TO ASK A SECOND, AND THE REASON IS NINE WORDS: I AM THE PERSON WHO MADE IT CHEAP.**

**THE SHELF, AND IT IS NOW A STATED FINDING RATHER THAN A HOOK. A REGISTER THAT GROWS WHEN NOBODY IS HURT IS A REGISTER SOMEBODY CAN HOLD OVER YOU, and a drawer of slips grows exactly the same way, out of correct things done by careful people, and nobody is hurt by it, and THE DIFFERENCE BETWEEN THE REGISTER AND THE DRAWER IS ONE LINE LONG: NOBODY EVER WROTE A RULE FOR THE PERSON. The register has a blank line under the nineteenth and no rule about who may write under the nineteenth. The drawer has no line. Not a blank one. A person has no line at all. It is going to be some poor devil of a frightened person in a kitchen, and somebody is going to put something on that table, and it is not going to be the bulb.`**

## Volume 03, Batch 0005 — Chapters 141–144 (VOLUME CLOSE)

**Window: Thursday 30 April 2015 to Wednesday 13 May 2015. Four chapters. THE SERIES IS AT 144 OF 720. VOLUME 03 IS CLOSED AND IT RAN TO CHAPTER 144, AS `outline/series.md` RESERVES. VOLUME 04 IS *EMERGENCY POWERS*, CHAPTERS 145–192, AND NOTHING IN THESE FOUR CHAPTERS MAY BE READ AS ANTICIPATING IT. `outline/volume-03.md` DOES NOT EXIST AND IS STILL OWED, AND NOTHING IN CHAPTERS 141–144 MAY BE BACK-FITTED INTO IT.**

**WHAT THIS CLOSE WAS FOR, AND WHAT IT DID, IN ONE PARAGRAPH. It was for the consequence of the three answers, of which nobody had yet been made to live under any, and it did the consequence in four different shapes — a person, a landing, a corridor, and a room — and in all four of them somebody was told a correct thing by a person who was not required to give it, and nobody was helped and nobody was harmed and nothing was written down. The register did not move once. There is no form. There is no chair. The volume ends where it began, with a blank line.**

**CHAPTER 0141, THURSDAY 30 APRIL — THE CONSEQUENCE OF THE FIRST ANSWER, AND IT IS A PERSON. A man of about forty-five with a cardboard wallet and three pieces of paper in it, who has never been in that vestibule in his life, asked at about a quarter past eleven whether there is a list, in FIVE QUESTIONS OF WHICH THE SHORTEST WAS THREE WORDS, and the counter was not allowed to give him one and did not and the refusal was correct, and NO DURATION IS GIVEN FOR IT AND NONE MAY BE, and it is the first time anybody has been told no at that counter about a piece of paper and the first time anybody has understood the why at the same time as the no. SHE COULD NOT TELL HIM WHOSE THEY ARE, COULD NOT IF SHE WANTED TO, WOULD NOT WORK IT OUT, AND WOULD NOT BE ASKED TO. There is no list of them and there is not a list of people, and she is not able to write one, and there is nowhere in this borough to write one, and if there were somewhere to write one then in about four years somebody would be able to look one up and the first thing anybody would do with it is find out whether a person is on it. He said `So they are not mine` and she said `They are not yours. And they are not not-yours, and that is the half I cannot help you with.` HE PUT THEM BACK IN THE INSIDE OF HIS COAT AND WENT OUT AND HE HAS BEEN CARRYING THEM SINCE ABOUT CHRISTMAS. THE FINDING: A STATE WITH NO OWNER AND NO PERSON ATTACHED TO IT HAS NOW BEEN CARRIED BY A MAN FOR ABOUT THREE MONTHS, AND NOBODY IN THIS CITY CAN TELL WHAT IT IS GOING TO BECOME, AND THE ANSWER IS NOTHING, BECAUSE THERE IS NO INSTRUMENT, AND THAT IS CORRECT. The other half of the chapter is the trough by the lock-ups: FOUR PIECES OF PAPER IN THIS BOROUGH MENTION IT, THREE OF THEM IN ONE INSIDE POCKET AND ONE IN ANOTHER, all of them true, and not one producible, and no way to ask which is the state of the trough because there are two men and they are both right and neither may be required to answer anything including a correction. A THING CAN BE LOOKED AT AGAIN AND A PERSON CANNOT, and he looked at it, and HE DID NOT WRITE A SECOND STATE, because a second state that did not agree would be a contradiction, and a contradiction can only be moved by asking the men, and the men may not be required to answer anything. And nobody in that room knows what day anybody comes, and the routine of the man with the bowser is a rota nobody wrote, and he tried three ways in April in a corridor and all three were closed, and he is not going to try again, and he would rather it stayed a coincidence.**

**CHAPTER 0142, FRIDAY 1 MAY — THE CONSEQUENCE OF THE SECOND ANSWER, AND THE PRICE ARRIVING ON A LANDING AND NOT AT A COUNTER. On a landing, at about a quarter to eleven, a man of about thirty with a plastic folder and a card of his own asked a woman of about sixty, in daylight, about eight words long, whether the board in the corridor at Saint Orra west is hers. THE ANSWER IS NO, AND THE REASON IT IS NO IS THAT NOBODY MAY BE REQUIRED TO BE TOLD ANYTHING, AND THE REASON THAT IS THE REASON IS THAT A PERSON WHO HAS BEEN ASKED ABOUT A JOB IS ON SOMETHING AND CANNOT GET OFF IT AGAIN, and because there is nowhere in this borough she could be put on a list, her absence is unrecordable, and the absence of her name is the only thing keeping her out of it. She could not give him anybody to give the card to. He went down the stairs with the card in his hand and left it on no mat and under no door, and nobody in that building was given a card about a floor being taken up on the Monday. A second person was on that landing and saw the whole of it and said nothing and has said nothing since, and that is all being seen to be asked is. **SHE IS NOT THE WOMAN OF ABOUT SIXTY WHO WAS IN THE VESTIBULE ON 27 APRIL 2015 WHO HAS READ A BOARD WITH AN EMPTY ROW ON IT EVERY MORNING FOR ELEVEN MONTHS. THEY ARE TWO PEOPLE AND THE TWO BOARDS ARE TWO BOARDS, and the chapter says so on the page at the point of introduction, and no later chapter may merge them.** JONAS MERCER WAS NOT IN IT AND WAS NOT GOING TO BE, and he was in that room on the Friday morning and came out of it knowing nothing about it, and nobody told him and nobody is going to, and the finding is that the whole of the price of findability is that it is paid by a person nobody is counting. THE SENTENCE OF THE THIRTEENTH OF MARCH WAS ABOUT FOUR PEOPLE. It is correct. There are more than four, and nobody in this borough has an instrument for saying how many more, and the number is not going to be arrived at by adding them up, because the first of them is not on a list either and neither is the fifth and there is nowhere to put the answer. The cost landed on her and not on him: on the Tuesday a man at the bins asked her who lived at number four B, and on the Thursday a girl on the landing asked her about the water, and the only way to stop being asked about the door is to stop being at the door, and the door is the only part of her day she has got, and nobody is entitled to ask her about the mornings.**

**CHAPTER 0143, SATURDAY 2 MAY MORNING — THE CONSEQUENCE OF THE THIRD ANSWER, COMING BACK FROM OUTSIDE THE BUILDING. A man of thirty-three who works nights and has been on nothing since he was twenty came in on a Saturday morning because a man at work told him there was a minute you could have for nothing, and he was given the minute of the twenty-seventh of April, which is public and unsigned and has no crest and requires nothing of anybody, and he read it standing up in a corridor and stopped on the fourth item and read it again, because in the middle of that item there is a sentence about what this floor is for and the sentence names a class of people and the class has no members in it that anybody can point at. HE USED IT CORRECTLY AND HE USED IT TO ASK A QUESTION IT WAS NEVER WRITTEN TO ANSWER: WHO IS IT FOR. HOW DO I GET TO BE ONE. And the answer, from a woman of about fifty who has been at that second-floor counter for eleven years and is not the woman who comes down to the ground floor at ten and at three, was correct in three halves. THE ONLY WAY SHE COULD PUT A PERSON INSIDE THAT SENTENCE IS BY MAKING A NEW SHEET, and anything new she writes has a box on it, and a box wants a name in it, and the day a name goes in a box in that building that person is on something and anybody can then ask them things and they cannot say no and mean it, because being on it will be a thing they did to themselves, in a room, in about eleven seconds, and they will have done it because they wanted help. THE MINUTE HAS NO BOX ON IT AND THAT IS NOT AN ACCIDENT AND SHE PUT IT THAT WAY HERSELF IN MARCH. And the other half, the half that took her eleven years at that counter, given for nothing on a Saturday morning: A SHEET WITH NO BOX ON IT IS A SHEET THAT A PERSON HAS TO PRODUCE ABOUT THEMSELVES, and the document is them, and there is nowhere on that sheet where them would go. A SENTENCE ABOUT WHO A FLOOR IS FOR IS NOT A ROUTE TO A FLOOR. It is not a list and it is not a queue and it is not a way in. Jonas Mercer was nine feet away at the wall for the whole of it and said nothing and was not asked anything and the man never knew he was there, and HE DID NOT SAY WHO HE WAS THINKING OF — he had got as far as a detail, on a day that was not this one, and had stopped, and he said `somebody I work with` instead, without appearing to notice that he had done it, and that is the first time in nine months Jonas Mercer has watched somebody not make a mistake. Nobody was helped, nobody was harmed, nothing was put in any document, and there is no minute of that morning. THE ONE LATTICE NOTICE IN THE WHOLE CLOSE, on that corridor wall, and it defines nothing and requires nothing: the record of this city is available to any person, free, and without a form, and anybody may have it by post or check it against the borough's own paper in person, and no person is asked for anything in order to have it and nothing on it requires any person to do anything.**

**CHAPTER 0144, SATURDAY 2 MAY AT TEN O'CLOCK AT NIGHT INTO WEDNESDAY 13 MAY — THE VOLUME'S LAST CHAPTER, AND THE ONLY CHAPTER IN WHICH A HANDOVER AND THE HOUR MAY BE STAGED, AND IT SAYS ON THE PAGE THAT BOTH ARE THE LAST TIME. THE FIFTEENTH, STAGED ONCE, at 22:00 on Saturday 2 May, in a waiting area with four chairs, a machine for hot water that has not worked since about August, and a clipboard with eleven rows and four written on in October by a man who then stopped, which is on the fourth chair and which NOBODY WROTE ON AND NOBODY HAS EVER WRITTEN ON, and which is not a log and was never a log. Ezra Mbeki and Trevor Nunn read a term of six days. Nothing was written down. There is no date on it and there has not been a date on one of these in this city since the twenty-fourth of January and there is not going to be one. Neither of them knows that a man refused out loud, in a corridor, in daylight, on 2 March, to be in that room, and neither is going to be told, and neither has ever been asked about any of them by anybody and neither ever will be, and the reason is not discretion — the reason is that there is nothing to ask about. HE WAS FOUR HUNDRED YARDS AWAY, ON PURPOSE, AND THE REASON HAS NOT CHANGED, and the gate went up at seven on the Sunday because two people who are not on anything had been in a room with four chairs the night before. THE HOUR BETWEEN SIX AND SEVEN, STAGED ONE LAST TIME on Sunday 3 May: nothing in this borough in force, no name for the hour, no date in this city for it, four hundred and twenty women not on the far side of a paragraph number, the empty row not empty, the register with no nineteenth entry, and a light in a car park he could not see into that was nothing. **AND ON THE PAGE: THIS IS THE LAST TIME EITHER OF THOSE TWO THINGS IS GOING TO BE STOOD IN BY ANYBODY.** He has stood in a corridor on a Sunday morning exactly twice and is not going to do it a third time; **AND HE HAD SAID AT THE END OF THE SATURDAY MORNING THAT HE WAS NOT GOING TO STAND IN THAT HOUR AGAIN, AND HE SAID IT THE WAY HE SAYS THE COUNTING, AND HE WENT ANYWAY, AND NOBODY SAW HIM AND NOBODY IS GOING TO BE TOLD AND HE WROTE NOTHING DOWN ABOUT IT, WHICH IS WHAT HE DOES — THE REVERSAL IS ON THE PAGE AND IS NOT SOFTENED**; he has stood on that wet pavement seven times by that night and eight by that morning, and he did the arithmetic and it came out the same as in April, and he is doing it anyway, because there is no rule that could stop him and no person who could tell him to, and he is thirty-two, and that is the last of it. The term that had been in force since seven on Sunday 3 May stopped at six on Sunday 10 May and another came in at seven, with no date on the night either was read on. SIXTEEN OF THOSE SATURDAY NIGHTS HAVE NOW BEEN REACHED AND NOT ONE HAS A DATE WRITTEN ON IT. ON WEDNESDAY 13 MAY, AT ABOUT TEN PAST TEN, IN THE VESTIBULE, HE SAID WHAT THE BOROUGH HAS, OUT LOUD, IN A ROOM, IN DAYLIGHT, WITHOUT ANSWERING ANYTHING AND WITHOUT IMPROVING ANYTHING, and the counter told him about a post and a trolley, and somebody said the only two words anybody said to him all day, `You are not on the agenda`, and he said `No`, and he got about half way through and let it come out badly, and he said the counter and the board and the card and the drawer and the tray and the two conditions and the compliance report and the nine seats and the six water points and a Saturday night with no date on it — AND THEN HE GOT TO THE PART WITH THE PEOPLE IN IT AND HE STOPPED, IN THE MIDDLE OF THAT ROOM, IN DAYLIGHT, AND HE SAID: `There is a place in that where the people go, and I am not going to say it, and that is not modesty. There is nowhere in this borough to put a person, so a room is the only place a person can be mentioned, and a room is exactly the place where it does not last.` Nobody wrote any of it down, nobody repeated it, nobody improved it, nobody has put it on a wall, it names no person, and nobody has been added to it and nobody has been taken off it, and he did not write it down himself because it is not his, and he was not thanked for anything and refused nothing of the kind. The counter did not answer him about a gate, or a rota, or a paragraph, or a person. And the standing of the city on the thirteenth of May, spoken, and then said plainly as the last movement of the volume, and it is not a list and it is not a roll-call and it may not be minuted, indexed, numbered or put on a wall, and it was not made that morning and it was not made that year. And the volume's last live sentence, which is a question and not a resolution, and which is not about a flood barrier or a school crossing or a citywide emergency, because that is Volume 04's material and it is not anticipated here: A SATURDAY NIGHT EVERY WEEK WITH NO DATE ON IT, A REGISTER WITH A BLANK LINE UNDER IT, ABOUT ELEVEN PEOPLE A DAY WALKING UNDER TWO CONDITIONS THAT DO NOT AGREE AND GETTING A DATE, AND A PUBLIC MINUTE THAT SAYS WHAT A SERVICE FLOOR IS FOR AND CANNOT SAY TO WHOM — AND NOBODY IN THIS BOROUGH HAS EVER YET WRITTEN DOWN WHO IS STANDING UNDER THE CONTRADICTION. SO WHO IS.**

**THE QUESTION COUNT FOR THE CLOSE, AND IT IS RIGHT IN EVERY CHAPTER: JONAS MERCER ASKED NO QUESTION OF ANYBODY IN ANY DAY OF THE FOUR CHAPTERS. He has now asked no question of anybody since the twenty-fifth of February 2015, and he had one question available to him in Chapter 0143, nine feet from a counter, on a Saturday morning, and did not spend it, and the chapter says so and says why.**
